op sum [3,4,0] [2,-1,0]  =>  [Resultant R = A + B] Write every vector in component form: \vec{A} = 3\hat{i} + 4\hat{j},\quad \vec{B} = 2\hat{i} - 1\hat{j} | Add the x-components together, the y-components together. | R_{x} = A_{x} + B_{x} = 3 + 2 = 5 | R_{y} = A_{y} + B_{y} = 4 + (-1) = 3 | Resultant vector: \vec{R} = 5\hat{i} + 3\hat{j} | Magnitude: R = \sqrt{R_{x}^2 + R_{y}^2} = \sqrt{5^2 + 3^2} = \sqrt{34} = 5.83 | Direction. First the reference angle, ignoring the signs: \theta_{ref} = \tan^{-1}\left(\frac{|R_{y}|}{|R_{x}|}\right) = \tan^{-1}\left(\frac{3}{5}\right) = 30.96^\circ | Rx is + and Ry is +, so the vector is in the 1st quadrant. \theta = \theta_{ref} = 30.96^\circ  =>  R = 5\hat{i} + 3\hat{j}, |R| = 5.83, θ = 30.96^\circ
op sub [3,4,0] [2,-1,0]  =>  [R = A − B] Subtracting a vector means adding its negative (same magnitude, opposite direction): \vec{A} - \vec{B} = \vec{A} + (-\vec{B}) | -\vec{B} = -2\hat{i} + 1\hat{j} | Add the x-components together, the y-components together. | R_{x} = A_{x} + (-B)_{x} = 3 + (-2) = 1 | R_{y} = A_{y} + (-B)_{y} = 4 + 1 = 5 | Resultant vector: \vec{R} = 1\hat{i} + 5\hat{j} | Magnitude: R = \sqrt{R_{x}^2 + R_{y}^2} = \sqrt{1^2 + 5^2} = \sqrt{26} = 5.1 | Direction. First the reference angle, ignoring the signs: \theta_{ref} = \tan^{-1}\left(\frac{|R_{y}|}{|R_{x}|}\right) = \tan^{-1}\left(\frac{5}{1}\right) = 78.69^\circ | Rx is + and Ry is +, so the vector is in the 1st quadrant. \theta = \theta_{ref} = 78.69^\circ  =>  R = 1\hat{i} + 5\hat{j}, |R| = 5.1, θ = 78.69^\circ
op dot [3,4,0] [2,-1,0]  =>  [Scalar product A·B] Given: \vec{A} = 3\hat{i} + 4\hat{j},\quad \vec{B} = 2\hat{i} - 1\hat{j} | Scalar product in terms of rectangular components: \vec{A}\cdot\vec{B} = A_{x}B_{x} + A_{y}B_{y} | = (3)(2) + (4)(-1) = 2 | Magnitudes: A = 5,\quad B = 2.24 | Angle between them: \cos\theta = \frac{\vec{A}\cdot\vec{B}}{A\,B} = \frac{2}{5\times2.23607} = 0.178885 | \theta = \cos^{-1}(0.178885) = 79.7^\circ | Meaning: A·B = |A| × (projection of B on A). B\cos\theta = 2.24\cos 79.7^\circ = 0.4  =>  A·B = 2, θ = 79.7^\circ
op cross [3,4,0] [2,-1,0]  =>  [Vector product A×B] Given: \vec{A} = 3\hat{i} + 4\hat{j},\quad \vec{B} = 2\hat{i} - 1\hat{j} | Write the cross product as a determinant: \vec{A}\times\vec{B} = \begin{vmatrix} \hat{i} & \hat{j} & \hat{k} \\ 3 & 4 & 0 \\ 2 & -1 & 0 \end{vmatrix} | Expand along the first row: = \hat{i}\,[(4)(0) - (0)(-1)] - \hat{j}\,[(3)(0) - (0)(2)] + \hat{k}\,[(3)(-1) - (4)(2)] | \vec{C} = -11\hat{k} | Magnitude: \left|\vec{C}\right| = \sqrt{0^2 + 0^2 + (-11)^2} = \sqrt{121} = 11 | Check with |A×B| = AB sin θ: 5\times2.236\times\sin 79.695^\circ = 11 | |A×B| is also the area of the parallelogram with sides A and B. \text{Area} = 11 | The direction is perpendicular to the plane of A and B (right-hand rule). The order matters: \vec{B}\times\vec{A} = -\vec{A}\times\vec{B} = 11\hat{k}  =>  A×B = -11\hat{k}, |A×B| = 11, θ = 79.7^\circ
op mag [3,4,0] [2,-1,0]  =>  [Magnitude and direction of A] Given components: \vec{A} = 3\hat{i} + 4\hat{j} | Magnitude: A = \sqrt{A_{x}^2 + A_{y}^2} = \sqrt{3^2 + 4^2} = \sqrt{25} = 5 | Direction. First the reference angle, ignoring the signs: \theta_{ref} = \tan^{-1}\left(\frac{|A_{y}|}{|A_{x}|}\right) = \tan^{-1}\left(\frac{4}{3}\right) = 53.13^\circ | Ax is + and Ay is +, so the vector is in the 1st quadrant. \theta = \theta_{ref} = 53.13^\circ  =>  |A| = 5, θ = 53.13^\circ
op resolve [3,4,0] [2,-1,0]  =>  [Resolve A into rectangular components] Given: magnitude A = 5 at θ = 53.13° with the +x axis. | x-component: A_{x} = A\cos\theta = 5\cos 53.13^\circ = 5\times 0.6 = 3 | y-component: A_{y} = A\sin\theta = 5\sin 53.13^\circ = 5\times 0.8 = 4 | Written with unit vectors: \vec{A} = 3\hat{i} + 4\hat{j}  =>  Ax = 3, Ay = 4
op scale [3,4,0] [2,-1,0]  =>  [R = 2A] Multiply every component by the scalar: 2\,\vec{A} = 2(3\hat{i} + 4\hat{j}) = 6\hat{i} + 8\hat{j} | The magnitude is multiplied by |k|: \left|2\,\vec{A}\right| = |2|\times 5 = 10 | k is positive, so the direction does not change.  =>  R = 6\hat{i} + 8\hat{j}, |R| = 10
op unit [3,4,0] [2,-1,0]  =>  [Unit vector along A] A unit vector has magnitude 1 and points the same way as the vector. \hat{a} = \frac{\vec{A}}{A} | A = 5 | \hat{a} = \frac{3\hat{i} + 4\hat{j}}{5} = 0.6\hat{i} + 0.8\hat{j} | Check: its magnitude is \sqrt{0.6^2 + 0.8^2} = 1  =>  unit vector = 0.6\hat{i} + 0.8\hat{j}
op angle [3,4,0] [2,-1,0]  =>  [Angle between A and B] Given: \vec{A} = 3\hat{i} + 4\hat{j},\quad \vec{B} = 2\hat{i} - 1\hat{j} | Scalar product in terms of rectangular components: \vec{A}\cdot\vec{B} = A_{x}B_{x} + A_{y}B_{y} | = (3)(2) + (4)(-1) = 2 | Magnitudes: A = 5,\quad B = 2.24 | Angle between them: \cos\theta = \frac{\vec{A}\cdot\vec{B}}{A\,B} = \frac{2}{5\times2.23607} = 0.178885 | \theta = \cos^{-1}(0.178885) = 79.7^\circ  =>  θ = 79.7^\circ
op proj [3,4,0] [2,-1,0]  =>  [Projection of B on A] Scalar projection of B on A (B cos θ): B\cos\theta = \frac{\vec{A}\cdot\vec{B}}{A} = \frac{2}{5} = 0.4 | Vector projection (the shadow of B along A): \text{proj}_{A}\vec{B} = \frac{\vec{A}\cdot\vec{B}}{A^2}\,\vec{A} = \frac{2}{25}(3\hat{i} + 4\hat{j}) = 0.24\hat{i} + 0.32\hat{j}  =>  B cos θ = 0.4, proj of B on A = 0.24\hat{i} + 0.32\hat{j}
op equil [3,4,0] [2,-1,0]  =>  [Force needed for equilibrium] Write every vector in component form: \vec{A} = 3\hat{i} + 4\hat{j},\quad \vec{B} = 2\hat{i} - 1\hat{j} | Add the x-components together, the y-components together. | R_{x} = A_{x} + B_{x} = 3 + 2 = 5 | R_{y} = A_{y} + B_{y} = 4 + (-1) = 3 | Resultant vector: \vec{R} = 5\hat{i} + 3\hat{j} | Magnitude: R = \sqrt{R_{x}^2 + R_{y}^2} = \sqrt{5^2 + 3^2} = \sqrt{34} = 5.83 | Direction. First the reference angle, ignoring the signs: \theta_{ref} = \tan^{-1}\left(\frac{|R_{y}|}{|R_{x}|}\right) = \tan^{-1}\left(\frac{3}{5}\right) = 30.96^\circ | Rx is + and Ry is +, so the vector is in the 1st quadrant. \theta = \theta_{ref} = 30.96^\circ | For equilibrium the net force must be zero, so the balancing force (equilibrant) is equal and opposite to R: \vec{E} = -\vec{R} = -5\hat{i} - 3\hat{j} | \left|\vec{E}\right| = 5.83  =>  E = -5\hat{i} - 3\hat{j}, |E| = 5.83
op torque [3,4,0] [2,-1,0]  =>  [Torque τ = r × F] Torque is the vector product of the position vector r and the force F. \vec{\tau} = \vec{r}\times\vec{F} | Given: \vec{r} = 3\hat{i} + 4\hat{j},\quad \vec{F} = 2\hat{i} - 1\hat{j} | Write the cross product as a determinant: \vec{r}\times\vec{F} = \begin{vmatrix} \hat{i} & \hat{j} & \hat{k} \\ 3 & 4 & 0 \\ 2 & -1 & 0 \end{vmatrix} | Expand along the first row: = \hat{i}\,[(4)(0) - (0)(-1)] - \hat{j}\,[(3)(0) - (0)(2)] + \hat{k}\,[(3)(-1) - (4)(2)] | \vec{τ} = -11\hat{k} | Magnitude: \left|\vec{τ}\right| = \sqrt{0^2 + 0^2 + (-11)^2} = \sqrt{121} = 11 | Check with |r×F| = rF sin θ: 5\times2.236\times\sin 79.695^\circ = 11 | |r×F| is also the area of the parallelogram with sides r and F. \text{Area} = 11 | The direction is perpendicular to the plane of r and F (right-hand rule). The order matters: \vec{F}\times\vec{r} = -\vec{r}\times\vec{F} = 11\hat{k}  =>  τ = -11\hat{k}\,\text{N m}, |τ| = 11\,\text{N m}, θ = 79.7^\circ
op work [3,4,0] [2,-1,0]  =>  [Work done W = F · d] Work is the scalar product of force and displacement: W = F·d = Fd cos θ. | Given: \vec{F} = 3\hat{i} + 4\hat{j},\quad \vec{d} = 2\hat{i} - 1\hat{j} | Scalar product in terms of rectangular components: \vec{F}\cdot\vec{d} = F_{x}d_{x} + F_{y}d_{y} | = (3)(2) + (4)(-1) = 2 | Magnitudes: F = 5,\quad d = 2.24 | Angle between them: \cos\theta = \frac{\vec{F}\cdot\vec{d}}{F\,d} = \frac{2}{5\times2.23607} = 0.178885 | \theta = \cos^{-1}(0.178885) = 79.7^\circ | W = 2\,\text{J}  =>  W = 2\,\text{J}, θ = 79.7^\circ
op lorentz [3,4,0] [2,-1,0]  =>  [Magnetic force F = q(v × B)] The force on a moving charge is F = q(v × B). First find v × B. | Given: \vec{v} = 3\hat{i} + 4\hat{j},\quad \vec{B} = 2\hat{i} - 1\hat{j} | Write the cross product as a determinant: \vec{v}\times\vec{B} = \begin{vmatrix} \hat{i} & \hat{j} & \hat{k} \\ 3 & 4 & 0 \\ 2 & -1 & 0 \end{vmatrix} | Expand along the first row: = \hat{i}\,[(4)(0) - (0)(-1)] - \hat{j}\,[(3)(0) - (0)(2)] + \hat{k}\,[(3)(-1) - (4)(2)] | \vec{v×B} = -11\hat{k} | Magnitude: \left|\vec{v×B}\right| = \sqrt{0^2 + 0^2 + (-11)^2} = \sqrt{121} = 11 | Multiply by the charge q = 1.6 × 10⁻¹⁹ C: \vec{F} = 1.6\times 10^{-19}\,(-11\hat{k}) = \left(-1.76\hat{k}\right)\times 10^{-18}\,\text{N} | \left|\vec{F}\right| = 1.76\times 10^{-18}\,\text{N}  =>  F = \left(-1.76\hat{k}\right)\times 10^{-18}\,\text{N}, |F| = 1.76\times 10^{-18}\,\text{N}
op relvel [3,4,0] [2,-1,0]  =>  [Velocity of A relative to B] Subtracting a vector means adding its negative (same magnitude, opposite direction): \vec{v_{A}} - \vec{v_{B}} = \vec{v_{A}} + (-\vec{v_{B}}) | -\vec{v_{B}} = -2\hat{i} + 1\hat{j} | Add the x-components together, the y-components together. | v_{AB,x} = v_{A,x} + (-v_{B})_{x} = 3 + (-2) = 1 | v_{AB,y} = v_{A,y} + (-v_{B})_{y} = 4 + 1 = 5 | Resultant vector: \vec{v_{AB}} = 1\hat{i} + 5\hat{j} | Magnitude: v_{AB} = \sqrt{v_{AB,x}^2 + v_{AB,y}^2} = \sqrt{1^2 + 5^2} = \sqrt{26} = 5.1 | Direction. op sum [3,4,5] [-1,2,2]  =>  [Resultant R = A + B] Write every vector in component form: \vec{A} = 3\hat{i} + 4\hat{j} + 5\hat{k},\quad \vec{B} = -1\hat{i} + 2\hat{j} + 2\hat{k} | Add the x-components together, the y-components together and the z-components together. | R_{x} = A_{x} + B_{x} = 3 + (-1) = 2 | R_{y} = A_{y} + B_{y} = 4 + 2 = 6 | R_{z} = A_{z} + B_{z} = 5 + 2 = 7 | Resultant vector: \vec{R} = 2\hat{i} + 6\hat{j} + 7\hat{k} | Magnitude (Pythagorean theorem in 3D): R = \sqrt{R_{x}^2 + R_{y}^2 + R_{z}^2} = \sqrt{2^2 + 6^2 + 7^2} = \sqrt{89} = 9.43 | Angle with the x-axis (direction cosine): \alpha = \cos^{-1}\left(\frac{R_{x}}{R}\right) = \cos^{-1}\left(\frac{2}{9.43}\right) = 77.76^\circ | Angle with the y-axis (direction cosine): \beta = \cos^{-1}\left(\frac{R_{y}}{R}\right) = \cos^{-1}\left(\frac{6}{9.434}\right) = 50.51^\circ | Angle with the z-axis (direction cosine): \gamma = \cos^{-1}\left(\frac{R_{z}}{R}\right) = \cos^{-1}\left(\frac{7}{9.434}\right) = 42.1^\circ  =>  R = 2\hat{i} + 6\hat{j} + 7\hat{k}, |R| = 9.43, α, β, γ = 77.76^\circ,\ 50.51^\circ,\ 42.1^\circ
op sub [3,4,5] [-1,2,2]  =>  [R = A − B] Subtracting a vector means adding its negative (same magnitude, opposite direction): \vec{A} - \vec{B} = \vec{A} + (-\vec{B}) | -\vec{B} = 1\hat{i} - 2\hat{j} - 2\hat{k} | Add the x-components together, the y-components together and the z-components together. | R_{x} = A_{x} + (-B)_{x} = 3 + 1 = 4 | R_{y} = A_{y} + (-B)_{y} = 4 + (-2) = 2 | R_{z} = A_{z} + (-B)_{z} = 5 + (-2) = 3 | Resultant vector: \vec{R} = 4\hat{i} + 2\hat{j} + 3\hat{k} | Magnitude (Pythagorean theorem in 3D): R = \sqrt{R_{x}^2 + R_{y}^2 + R_{z}^2} = \sqrt{4^2 + 2^2 + 3^2} = \sqrt{29} = 5.39 | Angle with the x-axis (direction cosine): \alpha = \cos^{-1}\left(\frac{R_{x}}{R}\right) = \cos^{-1}\left(\frac{4}{5.385}\right) = 42.03^\circ | Angle with the y-axis (direction cosine): \beta = \cos^{-1}\left(\frac{R_{y}}{R}\right) = \cos^{-1}\left(\frac{2}{5.385}\right) = 68.2^\circ | Angle with the z-axis (direction cosine): \gamma = \cos^{-1}\left(\frac{R_{z}}{R}\right) = \cos^{-1}\left(\frac{3}{5.3852}\right) = 56.15^\circ  =>  R = 4\hat{i} + 2\hat{j} + 3\hat{k}, |R| = 5.39, α, β, γ = 42.03^\circ,\ 68.2^\circ,\ 56.15^\circ
op dot [3,4,5] [-1,2,2]  =>  [Scalar product A·B] Given: \vec{A} = 3\hat{i} + 4\hat{j} + 5\hat{k},\quad \vec{B} = -1\hat{i} + 2\hat{j} + 2\hat{k} | Scalar product in terms of rectangular components: \vec{A}\cdot\vec{B} = A_{x}B_{x} + A_{y}B_{y} + A_{z}B_{z} | = (3)(-1) + (4)(2) + (5)(2) = 15 | Magnitudes: A = 7.07,\quad B = 3 | Angle between them: \cos\theta = \frac{\vec{A}\cdot\vec{B}}{A\,B} = \frac{15}{7.071\times3} = 0.7071 | \theta = \cos^{-1}(0.7071) = 45^\circ | Meaning: A·B = |A| × (projection of B on A). op cross [3,4,5] [-1,2,2]  =>  [Vector product A×B] Given: \vec{A} = 3\hat{i} + 4\hat{j} + 5\hat{k},\quad \vec{B} = -1\hat{i} + 2\hat{j} + 2\hat{k} | Write the cross product as a determinant: \vec{A}\times\vec{B} = \begin{vmatrix} \hat{i} & \hat{j} & \hat{k} \\ 3 & 4 & 5 \\ -1 & 2 & 2 \end{vmatrix} | Expand along the first row: = \hat{i}\,[(4)(2) - (5)(2)] - \hat{j}\,[(3)(2) - (5)(-1)] + \hat{k}\,[(3)(2) - (4)(-1)] | \vec{C} = -2\hat{i} - 11\hat{j} + 10\hat{k} | Magnitude: \left|\vec{C}\right| = \sqrt{(-2)^2 + (-11)^2 + 10^2} = \sqrt{225} = 15 | Check with |A×B| = AB sin θ: 7.07\times3\times\sin 45^\circ = 15 | |A×B| is also the area of the parallelogram with sides A and B. \text{Area} = 15 | The direction is perpendicular to the plane of A and B (right-hand rule). op proj [3,4,5] [-1,2,2]  =>  [Projection of B on A] Scalar projection of B on A (B cos θ): B\cos\theta = \frac{\vec{A}\cdot\vec{B}}{A} = \frac{15}{7.07} = 2.12 | Vector projection (the shadow of B along A): \text{proj}_{A}\vec{B} = \frac{\vec{A}\cdot\vec{B}}{A^2}\,\vec{A} = \frac{15}{50}(3\hat{i} + 4\hat{j} + 5\hat{k}) = 0.9\hat{i} + 1.2\hat{j} + 1.5\hat{k}  =>  B cos θ = 2.12, proj of B on A = 0.9\hat{i} + 1.2\hat{j} + 1.5\hat{k}
op equil [3,4,5] [-1,2,2]  =>  [Force needed for equilibrium] Write every vector in component form: \vec{A} = 3\hat{i} + 4\hat{j} + 5\hat{k},\quad \vec{B} = -1\hat{i} + 2\hat{j} + 2\hat{k} | Add the x-components together, the y-components together and the z-components together. | R_{x} = A_{x} + B_{x} = 3 + (-1) = 2 | R_{y} = A_{y} + B_{y} = 4 + 2 = 6 | R_{z} = A_{z} + B_{z} = 5 + 2 = 7 | Resultant vector: \vec{R} = 2\hat{i} + 6\hat{j} + 7\hat{k} | Magnitude (Pythagorean theorem in 3D): R = \sqrt{R_{x}^2 + R_{y}^2 + R_{z}^2} = \sqrt{2^2 + 6^2 + 7^2} = \sqrt{89} = 9.43 | Angle with the x-axis (direction cosine): \alpha = \cos^{-1}\left(\frac{R_{x}}{R}\right) = \cos^{-1}\left(\frac{2}{9.43}\right) = 77.76^\circ | Angle with the y-axis (direction cosine): \beta = \cos^{-1}\left(\frac{R_{y}}{R}\right) = \cos^{-1}\left(\frac{6}{9.434}\right) = 50.51^\circ | Angle with the z-axis (direction cosine): \gamma = \cos^{-1}\left(\frac{R_{z}}{R}\right) = \cos^{-1}\left(\frac{7}{9.434}\right) = 42.1^\circ | For equilibrium the net force must be zero, so the balancing force (equilibrant) is equal and opposite to R: \vec{E} = -\vec{R} = -2\hat{i} - 6\hat{j} - 7\hat{k} | \left|\vec{E}\right| = 9.43  =>  E = -2\hat{i} - 6\hat{j} - 7\hat{k}, |E| = 9.43
op torque [3,4,5] [-1,2,2]  =>  [Torque τ = r × F] Torque is the vector product of the position vector r and the force F. \vec{\tau} = \vec{r}\times\vec{F} | Given: \vec{r} = 3\hat{i} + 4\hat{j} + 5\hat{k},\quad \vec{F} = -1\hat{i} + 2\hat{j} + 2\hat{k} | Write the cross product as a determinant: \vec{r}\times\vec{F} = \begin{vmatrix} \hat{i} & \hat{j} & \hat{k} \\ 3 & 4 & 5 \\ -1 & 2 & 2 \end{vmatrix} | Expand along the first row: = \hat{i}\,[(4)(2) - (5)(2)] - \hat{j}\,[(3)(2) - (5)(-1)] + \hat{k}\,[(3)(2) - (4)(-1)] | \vec{τ} = -2\hat{i} - 11\hat{j} + 10\hat{k} | Magnitude: \left|\vec{τ}\right| = \sqrt{(-2)^2 + (-11)^2 + 10^2} = \sqrt{225} = 15 | Check with |r×F| = rF sin θ: 7.07\times3\times\sin 45^\circ = 15 | |r×F| is also the area of the parallelogram with sides r and F. \text{Area} = 15 | The direction is perpendicular to the plane of r and F (right-hand rule). The order matters: \vec{F}\times\vec{r} = -\vec{r}\times\vec{F} = 2\hat{i} + 11\hat{j} - 10\hat{k}  =>  τ = -2\hat{i} - 11\hat{j} + 10\hat{k}\,\text{N m}, |τ| = 15\,\text{N m}, θ = 45^\circ
op work [3,4,5] [-1,2,2]  =>  [Work done W = F · d] Work is the scalar product of force and displacement: W = F·d = Fd cos θ. | Given: \vec{F} = 3\hat{i} + 4\hat{j} + 5\hat{k},\quad \vec{d} = -1\hat{i} + 2\hat{j} + 2\hat{k} | Scalar product in terms of rectangular components: \vec{F}\cdot\vec{d} = F_{x}d_{x} + F_{y}d_{y} + F_{z}d_{z} | = (3)(-1) + (4)(2) + (5)(2) = 15 | Magnitudes: F = 7.07,\quad d = 3 | Angle between them: \cos\theta = \frac{\vec{F}\cdot\vec{d}}{F\,d} = \frac{15}{7.071\times3} = 0.7071 | \theta = \cos^{-1}(0.7071) = 45^\circ | W = 15\,\text{J}  =>  W = 15\,\text{J}, θ = 45^\circ
op lorentz [3,4,5] [-1,2,2]  =>  [Magnetic force F = q(v × B)] The force on a moving charge is F = q(v × B). First find v × B. | Given: \vec{v} = 3\hat{i} + 4\hat{j} + 5\hat{k},\quad \vec{B} = -1\hat{i} + 2\hat{j} + 2\hat{k} | Write the cross product as a determinant: \vec{v}\times\vec{B} = \begin{vmatrix} \hat{i} & \hat{j} & \hat{k} \\ 3 & 4 & 5 \\ -1 & 2 & 2 \end{vmatrix} | Expand along the first row: = \hat{i}\,[(4)(2) - (5)(2)] - \hat{j}\,[(3)(2) - (5)(-1)] + \hat{k}\,[(3)(2) - (4)(-1)] | \vec{v×B} = -2\hat{i} - 11\hat{j} + 10\hat{k} | Magnitude: \left|\vec{v×B}\right| = \sqrt{(-2)^2 + (-11)^2 + 10^2} = \sqrt{225} = 15 | Multiply by the charge q = 1.6 × 10⁻¹⁹ C: \vec{F} = 1.6\times 10^{-19}\,(-2\hat{i} - 11\hat{j} + 10\hat{k}) = \left(-0.32\hat{i} - 1.76\hat{j} + 1.6\hat{k}\right)\times 10^{-18}\,\text{N} | \left|\vec{F}\right| = 2.4\times 10^{-18}\,\text{N}  =>  F = \left(-0.32\hat{i} - 1.76\hat{j} + 1.6\hat{k}\right)\times 10^{-18}\,\text{N}, |F| = 2.4\times 10^{-18}\,\text{N}
op relvel [3,4,5] [-1,2,2]  =>  [Velocity of A relative to B] Subtracting a vector means adding its negative (same magnitude, opposite direction): \vec{v_{A}} - \vec{v_{B}} = \vec{v_{A}} + (-\vec{v_{B}}) | -\vec{v_{B}} = 1\hat{i} - 2\hat{j} - 2\hat{k} | Add the x-components together, the y-components together and the z-components together. | v_{AB,x} = v_{A,x} + (-v_{B})_{x} = 3 + 1 = 4 | v_{AB,y} = v_{A,y} + (-v_{B})_{y} = 4 + (-2) = 2 | v_{AB,z} = v_{A,z} + (-v_{B})_{z} = 5 + (-2) = 3 | Resultant vector: \vec{v_{AB}} = 4\hat{i} + 2\hat{j} + 3\hat{k} | Magnitude (Pythagorean theorem in 3D): v_{AB} = \sqrt{v_{AB,x}^2 + v_{AB,y}^2 + v_{AB,z}^2} = \sqrt{4^2 + 2^2 + 3^2} = \sqrt{29} = 5.39 | Angle with the x-axis (direction cosine): \alpha = \cos^{-1}\left(\frac{v_{AB,x}}{v_{AB}}\right) = \cos^{-1}\left(\frac{4}{5.385}\right) = 42.03^\circ | Angle with the y-axis (direction cosine): \beta = \cos^{-1}\left(\frac{v_{AB,y}}{v_{AB}}\right) = \cos^{-1}\left(\frac{2}{5.385}\right) = 68.2^\circ | Angle with the z-axis (direction cosine): \gamma = \cos^{-1}\left(\frac{v_{AB,z}}{v_{AB}}\right) = \cos^{-1}\left(\frac{3}{5.3852}\right) = 56.15^\circ  =>  v_AB = 4\hat{i} + 2\hat{j} + 3\hat{k}, |v_AB| = 5.39, α, β, γ = 42.03^\circ,\ 68.2^\circ,\ 56.15^\circ

B\cos\theta = 1\cos 90^\circ = 0  =>  A·B = 0, θ = 90^\circ
op cross [1,0,0] [0,1,0]  =>  [Vector product A×B] Given: \vec{A} = 1\hat{i},\quad \vec{B} = 1\hat{j} | Write the cross product as a determinant: \vec{A}\times\vec{B} = \begin{vmatrix} \hat{i} & \hat{j} & \hat{k} \\ 1 & 0 & 0 \\ 0 & 1 & 0 \end{vmatrix} | Expand along the first row: = \hat{i}\,[(0)(0) - (0)(1)] - \hat{j}\,[(1)(0) - (0)(0)] + \hat{k}\,[(1)(1) - (0)(0)] | \vec{C} = 1\hat{k} | Magnitude: \left|\vec{C}\right| = \sqrt{0^2 + 0^2 + 1^2} = \sqrt{1} = 1 | Check with |A×B| = AB sin θ: 1\times1\times\sin 90^\circ = 1 | |A×B| is also the area of the parallelogram with sides A and B. \text{Area} = 1 | The direction is perpendicular to the plane of A and B (right-hand rule). The order matters: \vec{B}\times\vec{A} = -\vec{A}\times\vec{B} = -1\hat{k}  =>  A×B = 1\hat{k}, |A×B| = 1, θ = 90^\circ
op mag [1,0,0] [0,1,0]  =>  [Magnitude and direction of A] Given components: \vec{A} = 1\hat{i} | Magnitude: A = \sqrt{A_{x}^2 + A_{y}^2} = \sqrt{1^2 + 0^2} = \sqrt{1} = 1 | Direction: Ay = 0, so the vector points straight along the +x axis. \theta = 0^\circ  =>  |A| = 1, θ = 0^\circ
op resolve [1,0,0] [0,1,0]  =>  [Resolve A into rectangular components] Given: magnitude A = 1 at θ = 0° with the +x axis. | x-component: A_{x} = A\cos\theta = 1\cos 0^\circ = 1\times 1 = 1 | y-component: A_{y} = A\sin\theta = 1\sin 0^\circ = 1\times 0 = 0 | Written with unit vectors: \vec{A} = 1\hat{i}  =>  Ax = 1, Ay = 0
op scale [1,0,0] [0,1,0]  =>  [R = 2A] Multiply every component by the scalar: 2\,\vec{A} = 2(1\hat{i}) = 2\hat{i} | The magnitude is multiplied by |k|: \left|2\,\vec{A}\right| = |2|\times 1 = 2 | k is positive, so the direction does not change.  =>  R = 2\hat{i}, |R| = 2
op unit [1,0,0] [0,1,0]  =>  [Unit vector along A] A unit vector has magnitude 1 and points the same way as the vector. \hat{a} = \frac{\vec{A}}{A} | A = 1 | \hat{a} = \frac{1\hat{i}}{1} = 1\hat{i} | Check: its magnitude is \sqrt{1^2 + 0^2} = 1  =>  unit vector = 1\hat{i}
op angle [1,0,0] [0,1,0]  =>  [Angle between A and B] Given: \vec{A} = 1\hat{i},\quad \vec{B} = 1\hat{j} | Scalar product in terms of rectangular components: \vec{A}\cdot\vec{B} = A_{x}B_{x} + A_{y}B_{y} | = (1)(0) + (0)(1) = 0 | Magnitudes: A = 1,\quad B = 1 | Angle between them: \cos\theta = \frac{\vec{A}\cdot\vec{B}}{A\,B} = \frac{0}{1\times1} = 0 | \theta = \cos^{-1}(0) = 90^\circ | The dot product is zero, so the vectors are perpendicular (θ = 90°).  =>  θ = 90^\circ
op proj [1,0,0] [0,1,0]  =>  [Projection of B on A] Scalar projection of B on A (B cos θ): B\cos\theta = \frac{\vec{A}\cdot\vec{B}}{A} = \frac{0}{1} = 0 | Vector projection (the shadow of B along A): \text{proj}_{A}\vec{B} = \frac{\vec{A}\cdot\vec{B}}{A^2}\,\vec{A} = \frac{0}{1}(1\hat{i}) = \vec{0}  =>  B cos θ = 0, proj of B on A = \vec{0}
op equil [1,0,0] [0,1,0]  =>  [Force needed for equilibrium] Write every vector in component form: \vec{A} = 1\hat{i},\quad \vec{B} = 1\hat{j} | Add the x-components together, the y-components together. | R_{x} = A_{x} + B_{x} = 1 + 0 = 1 | R_{y} = A_{y} + B_{y} = 0 + 1 = 1 | Resultant vector: \vec{R} = 1\hat{i} + 1\hat{j} | Magnitude: R = \sqrt{R_{x}^2 + R_{y}^2} = \sqrt{1^2 + 1^2} = \sqrt{2} = 1.41 | Direction. First the reference angle, ignoring the signs: \theta_{ref} = \tan^{-1}\left(\frac{|R_{y}|}{|R_{x}|}\right) = \tan^{-1}\left(\frac{1}{1}\right) = 45^\circ | Rx is + and Ry is +, so the vector is in the 1st quadrant. \theta = \theta_{ref} = 45^\circ | For equilibrium the net force must be zero, so the balancing force (equilibrant) is equal and opposite to R: \vec{E} = -\vec{R} = -1\hat{i} - 1\hat{j} | \left|\vec{E}\right| = 1.41  =>  E = -1\hat{i} - 1\hat{j}, |E| = 1.41
op torque [1,0,0] [0,1,0]  =>  [Torque τ = r × F] Torque is the vector product of the position vector r and the force F. \vec{\tau} = \vec{r}\times\vec{F} | Given: \vec{r} = 1\hat{i},\quad \vec{F} = 1\hat{j} | Write the cross product as a determinant: \vec{r}\times\vec{F} = \begin{vmatrix} \hat{i} & \hat{j} & \hat{k} \\ 1 & 0 & 0 \\ 0 & 1 & 0 \end{vmatrix} | Expand along the first row: = \hat{i}\,[(0)(0) - (0)(1)] - \hat{j}\,[(1)(0) - (0)(0)] + \hat{k}\,[(1)(1) - (0)(0)] | \vec{τ} = 1\hat{k} | Magnitude: \left|\vec{τ}\right| = \sqrt{0^2 + 0^2 + 1^2} = \sqrt{1} = 1 | Check with |r×F| = rF sin θ: 1\times1\times\sin 90^\circ = 1 | |r×F| is also the area of the parallelogram with sides r and F. \text{Area} = 1 | The direction is perpendicular to the plane of r and F (right-hand rule). The order matters: \vec{F}\times\vec{r} = -\vec{r}\times\vec{F} = -1\hat{k}  =>  τ = 1\hat{k}\,\text{N m}, |τ| = 1\,\text{N m}, θ = 90^\circ
op work [1,0,0] [0,1,0]  =>  [Work done W = F · d] Work is the scalar product of force and displacement: W = F·d = Fd cos θ. | Given: \vec{F} = 1\hat{i},\quad \vec{d} = 1\hat{j} | Scalar product in terms of rectangular components: \vec{F}\cdot\vec{d} = F_{x}d_{x} + F_{y}d_{y} | = (1)(0) + (0)(1) = 0 | Magnitudes: F = 1,\quad d = 1 | Angle between them: \cos\theta = \frac{\vec{F}\cdot\vec{d}}{F\,d} = \frac{0}{1\times1} = 0 | \theta = \cos^{-1}(0) = 90^\circ | The dot product is zero, so the vectors are perpendicular (θ = 90°). | W = 0\,\text{J}  =>  W = 0\,\text{J}, θ = 90^\circ
op lorentz [1,0,0] [0,1,0]  =>  [Magnetic force F = q(v × B)] The force on a moving charge is F = q(v × B). First find v × B. | Given: \vec{v} = 1\hat{i},\quad \vec{B} = 1\hat{j} | Write the cross product as a determinant: \vec{v}\times\vec{B} = \begin{vmatrix} \hat{i} & \hat{j} & \hat{k} \\ 1 & 0 & 0 \\ 0 & 1 & 0 \end{vmatrix} | Expand along the first row: = \hat{i}\,[(0)(0) - (0)(1)] - \hat{j}\,[(1)(0) - (0)(0)] + \hat{k}\,[(1)(1) - (0)(0)] | \vec{v×B} = 1\hat{k} | Magnitude: \left|\vec{v×B}\right| = \sqrt{0^2 + 0^2 + 1^2} = \sqrt{1} = 1 | Multiply by the charge q = 1.6 × 10⁻¹⁹ C: \vec{F} = 1.6\times 10^{-19}\,(1\hat{k}) = \left(1.6\hat{k}\right)\times 10^{-19}\,\text{N} | \left|\vec{F}\right| = 1.6\times 10^{-19}\,\text{N}  =>  F = \left(1.6\hat{k}\right)\times 10^{-19}\,\text{N}, |F| = 1.6\times 10^{-19}\,\text{N}
op relvel [1,0,0] [0,1,0]  =>  [Velocity of A relative to B] Subtracting a vector means adding its negative (same magnitude, opposite direction): \vec{v_{A}} - \vec{v_{B}} = \vec{v_{A}} + (-\vec{v_{B}}) | -\vec{v_{B}} = -1\hat{j} | Add the x-components together, the y-components together. | v_{AB,x} = v_{A,x} + (-v_{B})_{x} = 1 + 0 = 1 | v_{AB,y} = v_{A,y} + (-v_{B})_{y} = 0 + (-1) = -1 | Resultant vector: \vec{v_{AB}} = 1\hat{i} - 1\hat{j} | Magnitude: v_{AB} = \sqrt{v_{AB,x}^2 + v_{AB,y}^2} = \sqrt{1^2 + (-1)^2} = \sqrt{2} = 1.41 | Direction. First the reference angle, ignoring the signs: \theta_{ref} = \tan^{-1}\left(\frac{|v_{AB,y}|}{|v_{AB,x}|}\right) = \tan^{-1}\left(\frac{1}{1}\right) = 45^\circ | v_ABx is + and v_ABy is −, so the vector is in the 4th quadrant. \theta = 360^\circ - \theta_{ref} = 360^\circ - 45^\circ = 315^\circ  =>  v_AB = 1\hat{i} - 1\hat{j}, |v_AB| = 1.41, θ = 315^\circ

op dot [300,400,0] [-200,100,0]  =>  [Scalar product A·B] Given: \vec{A} = 300\hat{i} + 400\hat{j},\quad \vec{B} = -200\hat{i} + 100\hat{j} | Scalar product in terms of rectangular components: \vec{A}\cdot\vec{B} = A_{x}B_{x} + A_{y}B_{y} | = (300)(-200) + (400)(100) = -20000 | Magnitudes: A = 500,\quad B = 223.61 | Angle between them: \cos\theta = \frac{\vec{A}\cdot\vec{B}}{A\,B} = \frac{-20000}{500\times223.607} = -0.178885 | \theta = \cos^{-1}(-0.178885) = 100.3^\circ | Meaning: A·B = |A| × (projection of B on A). B\cos\theta = 223.607\cos 100.305^\circ = -40  =>  A·B = -20000, θ = 100.3^\circ
op cross [300,400,0] [-200,100,0]  =>  [Vector product A×B] Given: \vec{A} = 300\hat{i} + 400\hat{j},\quad \vec{B} = -200\hat{i} + 100\hat{j} | Write the cross product as a determinant: \vec{A}\times\vec{B} = \begin{vmatrix} \hat{i} & \hat{j} & \hat{k} \\ 300 & 400 & 0 \\ -200 & 100 & 0 \end{vmatrix} | Expand along the first row: = \hat{i}\,[(400)(0) - (0)(100)] - \hat{j}\,[(300)(0) - (0)(-200)] + \hat{k}\,[(300)(100) - (400)(-200)] | \vec{C} = 110000\hat{k} | Magnitude: \left|\vec{C}\right| = \sqrt{0^2 + 0^2 + 110000^2} = \sqrt{1.21\times 10^{10}} = 110000 | Check with |A×B| = AB sin θ: 500\times223.6068\times\sin 100.30485^\circ = 110000 | |A×B| is also the area of the parallelogram with sides A and B. \text{Area} = 110000 | The direction is perpendicular to the plane of A and B (right-hand rule). The order matters: \vec{B}\times\vec{A} = -\vec{A}\times\vec{B} = -110000\hat{k}  =>  A×B = 110000\hat{k}, |A×B| = 110000, θ = 100.3^\circ

op torque [0.00001,0,0] [0.0000070710678,0.0000070710678,0]  =>  [Torque τ = r × F] Torque is the vector product of the position vector r and the force F. \vec{\tau} = \vec{r}\times\vec{F} | Given: \vec{r} = 1\times 10^{-5}\hat{i},\quad \vec{F} = 7.07\times 10^{-6}\hat{i} + 7.07\times 10^{-6}\hat{j} | Write the cross product as a determinant: \vec{r}\times\vec{F} = \begin{vmatrix} \hat{i} & \hat{j} & \hat{k} \\ 1\times 10^{-5} & 0 & 0 \\ 7.07\times 10^{-6} & 7.07\times 10^{-6} & 0 \end{vmatrix} | Expand along the first row: = \hat{i}\,[(0)(0) - (0)(7.07\times 10^{-6})] - \hat{j}\,[(1\times 10^{-5})(0) - (0)(7.07\times 10^{-6})] + \hat{k}\,[(1\times 10^{-5})(7.07\times 10^{-6}) - (0)(7.07\times 10^{-6})] | \vec{τ} = 7.07\times 10^{-11}\hat{k} | Magnitude: \left|\vec{τ}\right| = \sqrt{0^2 + 0^2 + (7.07\times 10^{-11})^2} = \sqrt{5\times 10^{-21}} = 7.07\times 10^{-11} | Check with |r×F| = rF sin θ: 1\times 10^{-5}\times1\times 10^{-5}\times\sin 45^\circ = 7.07\times 10^{-11} | |r×F| is also the area of the parallelogram with sides r and F. \text{Area} = 7.07\times 10^{-11} | The direction is perpendicular to the plane of r and F (right-hand rule). The order matters: \vec{F}\times\vec{r} = -\vec{r}\times\vec{F} = -7.07\times 10^{-11}\hat{k}  =>  τ = 7.07\times 10^{-11}\hat{k}\,\text{N m}, |τ| = 7.07\times 10^{-11}\,\text{N m}, θ = 45^\circ
op work [0.00001,0,0] [0.0000070710678,0.0000070710678,0]  =>  [Work done W = F · d] Work is the scalar product of force and displacement: W = F·d = Fd cos θ. | Given: \vec{F} = 1\times 10^{-5}\hat{i},\quad \vec{d} = 7.07\times 10^{-6}\hat{i} + 7.07\times 10^{-6}\hat{j} | Scalar product in terms of rectangular components: \vec{F}\cdot\vec{d} = F_{x}d_{x} + F_{y}d_{y} | = (1\times 10^{-5})(7.07\times 10^{-6}) + (0)(7.07\times 10^{-6}) = 7.07\times 10^{-11} | Magnitudes: F = 1\times 10^{-5},\quad d = 1\times 10^{-5} | Angle between them: \cos\theta = \frac{\vec{F}\cdot\vec{d}}{F\,d} = \frac{7.071\times 10^{-11}}{1\times 10^{-5}\times1\times 10^{-5}} = 0.7071 | \theta = \cos^{-1}(0.7071) = 45^\circ | W = 7.07\times 10^{-11}\,\text{J}  =>  W = 7.07\times 10^{-11}\,\text{J}, θ = 45^\circ
op lorentz [0.00001,0,0] [0.0000070710678,0.0000070710678,0]  =>  [Magnetic force F = q(v × B)] The force on a moving charge is F = q(v × B). First find v × B. | Given: \vec{v} = 1\times 10^{-5}\hat{i},\quad \vec{B} = 7.07\times 10^{-6}\hat{i} + 7.07\times 10^{-6}\hat{j} | Write the cross product as a determinant: \vec{v}\times\vec{B} = \begin{vmatrix} \hat{i} & \hat{j} & \hat{k} \\ 1\times 10^{-5} & 0 & 0 \\ 7.07\times 10^{-6} & 7.07\times 10^{-6} & 0 \end{vmatrix} | Expand along the first row: = \hat{i}\,[(0)(0) - (0)(7.07\times 10^{-6})] - \hat{j}\,[(1\times 10^{-5})(0) - (0)(7.07\times 10^{-6})] + \hat{k}\,[(1\times 10^{-5})(7.07\times 10^{-6}) - (0)(7.07\times 10^{-6})] | \vec{v×B} = 7.07\times 10^{-11}\hat{k} | Magnitude: \left|\vec{v×B}\right| = \sqrt{0^2 + 0^2 + (7.07\times 10^{-11})^2} = \sqrt{5\times 10^{-21}} = 7.07\times 10^{-11} | Multiply by the charge q = 1.6 × 10⁻¹⁹ C: \vec{F} = 1.6\times 10^{-19}\,(7.07\times 10^{-11}\hat{k}) = \left(1.13\hat{k}\right)\times 10^{-29}\,\text{N} | \left|\vec{F}\right| = 1.13\times 10^{-29}\,\text{N}  =>  F = \left(1.13\hat{k}\right)\times 10^{-29}\,\text{N}, |F| = 1.13\times 10^{-29}\,\text{N}
op relvel [0.00001,0,0] [0.0000070710678,0.0000070710678,0]  =>  [Velocity of A relative to B] Subtracting a vector means adding its negative (same magnitude, opposite direction): \vec{v_{A}} - \vec{v_{B}} = \vec{v_{A}} + (-\vec{v_{B}}) | -\vec{v_{B}} = -7.07\times 10^{-6}\hat{i} - 7.07\times 10^{-6}\hat{j} | Add the x-components together, the y-components together. | v_{AB,x} = v_{A,x} + (-v_{B})_{x} = (1\times 10^{-5}) + (-7.07\times 10^{-6}) = 2.93\times 10^{-6} | v_{AB,y} = v_{A,y} + (-v_{B})_{y} = 0 + (-7.07\times 10^{-6}) = -7.07\times 10^{-6} | Resultant vector: \vec{v_{AB}} = 2.93\times 10^{-6}\hat{i} - 7.07\times 10^{-6}\hat{j} | Magnitude: v_{AB} = \sqrt{v_{AB,x}^2 + v_{AB,y}^2} = \sqrt{(2.929\times 10^{-6})^2 + (-7.071\times 10^{-6})^2} = \sqrt{5.858\times 10^{-11}} = 7.65\times 10^{-6} | Direction. First the reference angle, ignoring the signs: \theta_{ref} = \tan^{-1}\left(\frac{|v_{AB,y}|}{|v_{AB,x}|}\right) = \tan^{-1}\left(\frac{7.071\times 10^{-6}}{2.929\times 10^{-6}}\right) = 67.5^\circ | v_ABx is + and v_ABy is −, so the vector is in the 4th quadrant. \theta = 360^\circ - \theta_{ref} = 360^\circ - 67.5^\circ = 292.5^\circ  =>  v_AB = 2.93\times 10^{-6}\hat{i} - 7.07\times 10^{-6}\hat{j}, |v_AB| = 7.65\times 10^{-6}, θ = 292.5^\circ

op sum [0.00001,0,0] [0,0.00001,0]  =>  [Resultant R = A + B] Write every vector in component form: \vec{A} = 1\times 10^{-5}\hat{i},\quad \vec{B} = 1\times 10^{-5}\hat{j} | Add the x-components together, the y-components together. | R_{x} = A_{x} + B_{x} = (1\times 10^{-5}) + 0 = 1\times 10^{-5} | R_{y} = A_{y} + B_{y} = 0 + (1\times 10^{-5}) = 1\times 10^{-5} | Resultant vector: \vec{R} = 1\times 10^{-5}\hat{i} + 1\times 10^{-5}\hat{j} | Magnitude: R = \sqrt{R_{x}^2 + R_{y}^2} = \sqrt{(1\times 10^{-5})^2 + (1\times 10^{-5})^2} = \sqrt{2\times 10^{-10}} = 1.41\times 10^{-5} | Direction. op dot [0.00001,0,0] [0,0.00001,0]  =>  [Scalar product A·B] Given: \vec{A} = 1\times 10^{-5}\hat{i},\quad \vec{B} = 1\times 10^{-5}\hat{j} | Scalar product in terms of rectangular components: \vec{A}\cdot\vec{B} = A_{x}B_{x} + A_{y}B_{y} | = (1\times 10^{-5})(0) + (0)(1\times 10^{-5}) = 0 | Magnitudes: A = 1\times 10^{-5},\quad B = 1\times 10^{-5} | Angle between them: \cos\theta = \frac{\vec{A}\cdot\vec{B}}{A\,B} = \frac{0}{1\times 10^{-5}\times1\times 10^{-5}} = 0 | \theta = \cos^{-1}(0) = 90^\circ | The dot product is zero, so the vectors are perpendicular (θ = 90°). | Meaning: A·B = |A| × (projection of B on A). op cross [0.00001,0,0] [0,0.00001,0]  =>  [Vector product A×B] Given: \vec{A} = 1\times 10^{-5}\hat{i},\quad \vec{B} = 1\times 10^{-5}\hat{j} | Write the cross product as a determinant: \vec{A}\times\vec{B} = \begin{vmatrix} \hat{i} & \hat{j} & \hat{k} \\ 1\times 10^{-5} & 0 & 0 \\ 0 & 1\times 10^{-5} & 0 \end{vmatrix} | Expand along the first row: = \hat{i}\,[(0)(0) - (0)(1\times 10^{-5})] - \hat{j}\,[(1\times 10^{-5})(0) - (0)(0)] + \hat{k}\,[(1\times 10^{-5})(1\times 10^{-5}) - (0)(0)] | \vec{C} = 1\times 10^{-10}\hat{k} | Magnitude: \left|\vec{C}\right| = \sqrt{0^2 + 0^2 + (1\times 10^{-10})^2} = \sqrt{1\times 10^{-20}} = 1\times 10^{-10} | Check with |A×B| = AB sin θ: 1\times 10^{-5}\times1\times 10^{-5}\times\sin 90^\circ = 1\times 10^{-10} | |A×B| is also the area of the parallelogram with sides A and B. \text{Area} = 1\times 10^{-10} | The direction is perpendicular to the plane of A and B (right-hand rule). The order matters: \vec{B}\times\vec{A} = -\vec{A}\times\vec{B} = -1\times 10^{-10}\hat{k}  =>  A×B = 1\times 10^{-10}\hat{k}, |A×B| = 1\times 10^{-10}, θ = 90^\circ

op resolve [0.00001,0,0] [0,0.00001,0]  =>  [Resolve A into rectangular components] Given: magnitude A = 1 × 10⁻⁵ at θ = 0° with the +x axis. | x-component: A_{x} = A\cos\theta = 1\times 10^{-5}\cos 0^\circ = 1\times 10^{-5}\times 1 = 1\times 10^{-5} | y-component: A_{y} = A\sin\theta = 1\times 10^{-5}\sin 0^\circ = 1\times 10^{-5}\times 0 = 0 | Written with unit vectors: \vec{A} = 1\times 10^{-5}\hat{i}  =>  Ax = 1\times 10^{-5}, Ay = 0

op unit [0.00001,0,0] [0,0.00001,0]  =>  [Unit vector along A] A unit vector has magnitude 1 and points the same way as the vector. \hat{a} = \frac{\vec{A}}{A} | A = 1\times 10^{-5} | \hat{a} = \frac{1\times 10^{-5}\hat{i}}{1\times 10^{-5}} = 1\hat{i} | Check: its magnitude is \sqrt{1^2 + 0^2} = 1  =>  unit vector = 1\hat{i}

op dot [0,0,0] [3,4,0]  =>  [Scalar product A·B] Given: \vec{A} = \vec{0},\quad \vec{B} = 3\hat{i} + 4\hat{j} | A has zero length, so A·B = 0 and there is no angle between them. \vec{A}\cdot\vec{B} = 0  =>  A·B = 0, θ = \text{undefined}
op cross [0,0,0] [3,4,0]  =>  [Vector product A×B] Given: \vec{A} = \vec{0},\quad \vec{B} = 3\hat{i} + 4\hat{j} | Write the cross product as a determinant: \vec{A}\times\vec{B} = \begin{vmatrix} \hat{i} & \hat{j} & \hat{k} \\ 0 & 0 & 0 \\ 3 & 4 & 0 \end{vmatrix} | Expand along the first row: = \hat{i}\,[(0)(0) - (0)(4)] - \hat{j}\,[(0)(0) - (0)(3)] + \hat{k}\,[(0)(4) - (0)(3)] | \vec{C} = \vec{0} | Magnitude: \left|\vec{C}\right| = \sqrt{0^2 + 0^2 + 0^2} = \sqrt{0} = 0 | A has zero length, so A×B is the null vector and there is no angle between them. | |A×B| is also the area of the parallelogram with sides A and B. \text{Area} = 0 | A×B is the null vector, which has no direction. Swapping the order changes nothing: \vec{B}\times\vec{A} = -\vec{A}\times\vec{B} = \vec{0}  =>  A×B = \vec{0}, |A×B| = 0, θ = \text{undefined}
op mag [0,0,0] [3,4,0]  =>  [Magnitude and direction of A] Given components: \vec{A} = \vec{0} | Magnitude: A = \sqrt{A_{x}^2 + A_{y}^2} = \sqrt{0^2 + 0^2} = \sqrt{0} = 0 | Direction: the vector has zero length, so its direction is undefined.  =>  |A| = 0, θ = \text{undefined}
op resolve [0,0,0] [3,4,0]  =>  [Resolve A into rectangular components] Given: magnitude A = 0 at θ = 0° with the +x axis. | x-component: A_{x} = A\cos\theta = 0\cos 0^\circ = 0\times 1 = 0 | y-component: A_{y} = A\sin\theta = 0\sin 0^\circ = 0\times 0 = 0 | Written with unit vectors: \vec{A} = \vec{0}  =>  Ax = 0, Ay = 0
op scale [0,0,0] [3,4,0]  =>  [R = 2A] Multiply every component by the scalar: 2\,\vec{A} = 2(\vec{0}) = \vec{0} | The magnitude is multiplied by |k|: \left|2\,\vec{A}\right| = |2|\times 0 = 0 | k is positive, so the direction does not change.  =>  R = \vec{0}, |R| = 0
op unit [0,0,0] [3,4,0]  =>  [Unit vector along A] A unit vector has magnitude 1 and points the same way as the vector. \hat{a} = \frac{\vec{A}}{A} | A = 0 | A has zero length, so it has no direction and there is no unit vector along it (that would mean dividing by zero).  =>  unit vector = \text{none: a zero vector has no direction}
op angle [0,0,0] [3,4,0]  =>  [Angle between A and B] Given: \vec{A} = \vec{0},\quad \vec{B} = 3\hat{i} + 4\hat{j} | A has zero length, so A·B = 0 and there is no angle between them. \vec{A}\cdot\vec{B} = 0  =>  θ = \text{undefined}
op proj [0,0,0] [3,4,0]  =>  [Projection of B on A] A has zero length, so there is no direction to project B onto.  =>  B cos θ = \text{undefined}, proj of B on A = \text{undefined}
op equil [0,0,0] [3,4,0]  =>  [Force needed for equilibrium] Write every vector in component form: \vec{A} = \vec{0},\quad \vec{B} = 3\hat{i} + 4\hat{j} | Add the x-components together, the y-components together. | R_{x} = A_{x} + B_{x} = 0 + 3 = 3 | R_{y} = A_{y} + B_{y} = 0 + 4 = 4 | Resultant vector: \vec{R} = 3\hat{i} + 4\hat{j} | Magnitude: R = \sqrt{R_{x}^2 + R_{y}^2} = \sqrt{3^2 + 4^2} = \sqrt{25} = 5 | Direction. First the reference angle, ignoring the signs: \theta_{ref} = \tan^{-1}\left(\frac{|R_{y}|}{|R_{x}|}\right) = \tan^{-1}\left(\frac{4}{3}\right) = 53.13^\circ | Rx is + and Ry is +, so the vector is in the 1st quadrant. \theta = \theta_{ref} = 53.13^\circ | For equilibrium the net force must be zero, so the balancing force (equilibrant) is equal and opposite to R: \vec{E} = -\vec{R} = -3\hat{i} - 4\hat{j} | \left|\vec{E}\right| = 5  =>  E = -3\hat{i} - 4\hat{j}, |E| = 5
op torque [0,0,0] [3,4,0]  =>  [Torque τ = r × F] Torque is the vector product of the position vector r and the force F. \vec{\tau} = \vec{r}\times\vec{F} | Given: \vec{r} = \vec{0},\quad \vec{F} = 3\hat{i} + 4\hat{j} | Write the cross product as a determinant: \vec{r}\times\vec{F} = \begin{vmatrix} \hat{i} & \hat{j} & \hat{k} \\ 0 & 0 & 0 \\ 3 & 4 & 0 \end{vmatrix} | Expand along the first row: = \hat{i}\,[(0)(0) - (0)(4)] - \hat{j}\,[(0)(0) - (0)(3)] + \hat{k}\,[(0)(4) - (0)(3)] | \vec{τ} = \vec{0} | Magnitude: \left|\vec{τ}\right| = \sqrt{0^2 + 0^2 + 0^2} = \sqrt{0} = 0 | r has zero length, so r×F is the null vector and there is no angle between them. | |r×F| is also the area of the parallelogram with sides r and F. \text{Area} = 0 | r×F is the null vector, which has no direction. Swapping the order changes nothing: \vec{F}\times\vec{r} = -\vec{r}\times\vec{F} = \vec{0}  =>  τ = \vec{0}\,\text{N m}, |τ| = 0\,\text{N m}, θ = \text{undefined}
op work [0,0,0] [3,4,0]  =>  [Work done W = F · d] Work is the scalar product of force and displacement: W = F·d = Fd cos θ. | Given: \vec{F} = \vec{0},\quad \vec{d} = 3\hat{i} + 4\hat{j} | F has zero length, so F·d = 0 and there is no angle between them. \vec{F}\cdot\vec{d} = 0 | W = 0\,\text{J}  =>  W = 0\,\text{J}, θ = \text{undefined}
op lorentz [0,0,0] [3,4,0]  =>  [Magnetic force F = q(v × B)] The force on a moving charge is F = q(v × B). First find v × B. | Given: \vec{v} = \vec{0},\quad \vec{B} = 3\hat{i} + 4\hat{j} | Write the cross product as a determinant: \vec{v}\times\vec{B} = \begin{vmatrix} \hat{i} & \hat{j} & \hat{k} \\ 0 & 0 & 0 \\ 3 & 4 & 0 \end{vmatrix} | Expand along the first row: = \hat{i}\,[(0)(0) - (0)(4)] - \hat{j}\,[(0)(0) - (0)(3)] + \hat{k}\,[(0)(4) - (0)(3)] | \vec{v×B} = \vec{0} | Magnitude: \left|\vec{v×B}\right| = \sqrt{0^2 + 0^2 + 0^2} = \sqrt{0} = 0 | Multiply by the charge q = 1.6 × 10⁻¹⁹ C: \vec{F} = 1.6\times 10^{-19}\,(\vec{0}) = \vec{0}\,\text{N} | \left|\vec{F}\right| = 0\,\text{N}  =>  F = \vec{0}\,\text{N}, |F| = 0\,\text{N}
op relvel [0,0,0] [3,4,0]  =>  [Velocity of A relative to B] Subtracting a vector means adding its negative (same magnitude, opposite direction): \vec{v_{A}} - \vec{v_{B}} = \vec{v_{A}} + (-\vec{v_{B}}) | -\vec{v_{B}} = -3\hat{i} - 4\hat{j} | Add the x-components together, the y-components together. | v_{AB,x} = v_{A,x} + (-v_{B})_{x} = 0 + (-3) = -3 | v_{AB,y} = v_{A,y} + (-v_{B})_{y} = 0 + (-4) = -4 | Resultant vector: \vec{v_{AB}} = -3\hat{i} - 4\hat{j} | Magnitude: v_{AB} = \sqrt{v_{AB,x}^2 + v_{AB,y}^2} = \sqrt{(-3)^2 + (-4)^2} = \sqrt{25} = 5 | Direction. First the reference angle, ignoring the signs: \theta_{ref} = \tan^{-1}\left(\frac{|v_{AB,y}|}{|v_{AB,x}|}\right) = \tan^{-1}\left(\frac{4}{3}\right) = 53.13^\circ | v_ABx is − and v_ABy is −, so the vector is in the 3rd quadrant. \theta = 180^\circ + \theta_{ref} = 180^\circ + 53.13^\circ = 233.13^\circ  =>  v_AB = -3\hat{i} - 4\hat{j}, |v_AB| = 5, θ = 233.13^\circ

op sum [1e-7,0,0] [7.0710678e-8,7.0710678e-8,0]  =>  [Resultant R = A + B] Write every vector in component form: \vec{A} = 1\times 10^{-7}\hat{i},\quad \vec{B} = 7.07\times 10^{-8}\hat{i} + 7.07\times 10^{-8}\hat{j} | Add the x-components together, the y-components together. | R_{x} = A_{x} + B_{x} = (1\times 10^{-7}) + (7.07\times 10^{-8}) = 1.71\times 10^{-7} | R_{y} = A_{y} + B_{y} = 0 + (7.07\times 10^{-8}) = 7.07\times 10^{-8} | Resultant vector: \vec{R} = 1.71\times 10^{-7}\hat{i} + 7.07\times 10^{-8}\hat{j} | Magnitude: R = \sqrt{R_{x}^2 + R_{y}^2} = \sqrt{(1.707\times 10^{-7})^2 + (7.071\times 10^{-8})^2} = \sqrt{3.41\times 10^{-14}} = 1.85\times 10^{-7} | Direction. op dot [1e-7,0,0] [7.0710678e-8,7.0710678e-8,0]  =>  [Scalar product A·B] Given: \vec{A} = 1\times 10^{-7}\hat{i},\quad \vec{B} = 7.07\times 10^{-8}\hat{i} + 7.07\times 10^{-8}\hat{j} | Scalar product in terms of rectangular components: \vec{A}\cdot\vec{B} = A_{x}B_{x} + A_{y}B_{y} | = (1\times 10^{-7})(7.07\times 10^{-8}) + (0)(7.07\times 10^{-8}) = 7.07\times 10^{-15} | Magnitudes: A = 1\times 10^{-7},\quad B = 1\times 10^{-7} | Angle between them: \cos\theta = \frac{\vec{A}\cdot\vec{B}}{A\,B} = \frac{7.071\times 10^{-15}}{1\times 10^{-7}\times1\times 10^{-7}} = 0.7071 | \theta = \cos^{-1}(0.7071) = 45^\circ | Meaning: A·B = |A| × (projection of B on A). op lorentz [1e-7,0,0] [7.0710678e-8,7.0710678e-8,0]  =>  [Magnetic force F = q(v × B)] The force on a moving charge is F = q(v × B). First find v × B. | Given: \vec{v} = 1\times 10^{-7}\hat{i},\quad \vec{B} = 7.07\times 10^{-8}\hat{i} + 7.07\times 10^{-8}\hat{j} | Write the cross product as a determinant: \vec{v}\times\vec{B} = \begin{vmatrix} \hat{i} & \hat{j} & \hat{k} \\ 1\times 10^{-7} & 0 & 0 \\ 7.07\times 10^{-8} & 7.07\times 10^{-8} & 0 \end{vmatrix} | Expand along the first row: = \hat{i}\,[(0)(0) - (0)(7.07\times 10^{-8})] - \hat{j}\,[(1\times 10^{-7})(0) - (0)(7.07\times 10^{-8})] + \hat{k}\,[(1\times 10^{-7})(7.07\times 10^{-8}) - (0)(7.07\times 10^{-8})] | \vec{v×B} = \left(7.07\hat{k}\right)\times 10^{-15} | Magnitude: \left|\vec{v×B}\right| = \sqrt{0^2 + 0^2 + (7.07\times 10^{-15})^2} = \sqrt{5\times 10^{-29}} = 7.07\times 10^{-15} | Multiply by the charge q = 1.6 × 10⁻¹⁹ C: \vec{F} = 1.6\times 10^{-19}\,(\left(7.07\hat{k}\right)\times 10^{-15}) = \left(1.13\hat{k}\right)\times 10^{-33}\,\text{N} | \left|\vec{F}\right| = 1.13\times 10^{-33}\,\text{N}  =>  F = \left(1.13\hat{k}\right)\times 10^{-33}\,\text{N}, |F| = 1.13\times 10^{-33}\,\text{N}
op relvel [1e-7,0,0] [7.0710678e-8,7.0710678e-8,0]  =>  [Velocity of A relative to B] Subtracting a vector means adding its negative (same magnitude, opposite direction): \vec{v_{A}} - \vec{v_{B}} = \vec{v_{A}} + (-\vec{v_{B}}) | -\vec{v_{B}} = -7.07\times 10^{-8}\hat{i} - 7.07\times 10^{-8}\hat{j} | Add the x-components together, the y-components together. | v_{AB,x} = v_{A,x} + (-v_{B})_{x} = (1\times 10^{-7}) + (-7.07\times 10^{-8}) = 2.93\times 10^{-8} | v_{AB,y} = v_{A,y} + (-v_{B})_{y} = 0 + (-7.07\times 10^{-8}) = -7.07\times 10^{-8} | Resultant vector: \vec{v_{AB}} = 2.93\times 10^{-8}\hat{i} - 7.07\times 10^{-8}\hat{j} | Magnitude: v_{AB} = \sqrt{v_{AB,x}^2 + v_{AB,y}^2} = \sqrt{(2.929\times 10^{-8})^2 + (-7.071\times 10^{-8})^2} = \sqrt{5.858\times 10^{-15}} = 7.65\times 10^{-8} | Direction. First the reference angle, ignoring the signs: \theta_{ref} = \tan^{-1}\left(\frac{|v_{AB,y}|}{|v_{AB,x}|}\right) = \tan^{-1}\left(\frac{7.071\times 10^{-8}}{2.929\times 10^{-8}}\right) = 67.5^\circ | v_ABx is + and v_ABy is −, so the vector is in the 4th quadrant. \theta = 360^\circ - \theta_{ref} = 360^\circ - 67.5^\circ = 292.5^\circ  =>  v_AB = 2.93\times 10^{-8}\hat{i} - 7.07\times 10^{-8}\hat{j}, |v_AB| = 7.65\times 10^{-8}, θ = 292.5^\circ

op angle [-500000,866025.40378,0] [866025.40378,500000,0]  =>  [Angle between A and B] Given: \vec{A} = -500000\hat{i} + 866025.4\hat{j},\quad \vec{B} = 866025.4\hat{i} + 500000\hat{j} | Scalar product in terms of rectangular components: \vec{A}\cdot\vec{B} = A_{x}B_{x} + A_{y}B_{y} | = (-500000)(866025.4) + (866025.4)(500000) = 0 | Magnitudes: A = 1000000,\quad B = 1000000 | Angle between them: \cos\theta = \frac{\vec{A}\cdot\vec{B}}{A\,B} = \frac{0}{1000000\times1000000} = 0 | \theta = \cos^{-1}(0) = 90^\circ | The dot product is zero, so the vectors are perpendicular (θ = 90°).  =>  θ = 90^\circ

op equil [-500000,866025.40378,0] [866025.40378,500000,0]  =>  [Force needed for equilibrium] Write every vector in component form: \vec{A} = -500000\hat{i} + 866025.4\hat{j},\quad \vec{B} = 866025.4\hat{i} + 500000\hat{j} | Add the x-components together, the y-components together. | R_{x} = A_{x} + B_{x} = (-500000) + 866025.4 = 366025.4 | R_{y} = A_{y} + B_{y} = 866025.4 + 500000 = 1366025.4 | Resultant vector: \vec{R} = 366025.4\hat{i} + 1366025.4\hat{j} | Magnitude: R = \sqrt{R_{x}^2 + R_{y}^2} = \sqrt{366025.4^2 + 1366025.4^2} = \sqrt{2\times 10^{12}} = 1414213.56 | Direction. First the reference angle, ignoring the signs: \theta_{ref} = \tan^{-1}\left(\frac{|R_{y}|}{|R_{x}|}\right) = \tan^{-1}\left(\frac{1366025.4}{366025.4}\right) = 75^\circ | Rx is + and Ry is +, so the vector is in the 1st quadrant. \theta = \theta_{ref} = 75^\circ | For equilibrium the net force must be zero, so the balancing force (equilibrant) is equal and opposite to R: \vec{E} = -\vec{R} = -366025.4\hat{i} - 1366025.4\hat{j} | \left|\vec{E}\right| = 1414213.56  =>  E = -366025.4\hat{i} - 1366025.4\hat{j}, |E| = 1414213.56
op torque [-500000,866025.40378,0] [866025.40378,500000,0]  =>  [Torque τ = r × F] Torque is the vector product of the position vector r and the force F. \vec{\tau} = \vec{r}\times\vec{F} | Given: \vec{r} = -500000\hat{i} + 866025.4\hat{j},\quad \vec{F} = 866025.4\hat{i} + 500000\hat{j} | Write the cross product as a determinant: \vec{r}\times\vec{F} = \begin{vmatrix} \hat{i} & \hat{j} & \hat{k} \\ -500000 & 866025.4 & 0 \\ 866025.4 & 500000 & 0 \end{vmatrix} | Expand along the first row: = \hat{i}\,[(866025.4)(0) - (0)(500000)] - \hat{j}\,[(-500000)(0) - (0)(866025.4)] + \hat{k}\,[(-500000)(500000) - (866025.4)(866025.4)] | \vec{τ} = -1\times 10^{12}\hat{k} | Magnitude: \left|\vec{τ}\right| = \sqrt{0^2 + 0^2 + (-1\times 10^{12})^2} = \sqrt{1\times 10^{24}} = 1\times 10^{12} | Check with |r×F| = rF sin θ: 1000000\times1000000\times\sin 90^\circ = 1\times 10^{12} | |r×F| is also the area of the parallelogram with sides r and F. \text{Area} = 1\times 10^{12} | The direction is perpendicular to the plane of r and F (right-hand rule). The order matters: \vec{F}\times\vec{r} = -\vec{r}\times\vec{F} = 1\times 10^{12}\hat{k}  =>  τ = -1\times 10^{12}\hat{k}\,\text{N m}, |τ| = 1\times 10^{12}\,\text{N m}, θ = 90^\circ
op work [-500000,866025.40378,0] [866025.40378,500000,0]  =>  [Work done W = F · d] Work is the scalar product of force and displacement: W = F·d = Fd cos θ. | Given: \vec{F} = -500000\hat{i} + 866025.4\hat{j},\quad \vec{d} = 866025.4\hat{i} + 500000\hat{j} | Scalar product in terms of rectangular components: \vec{F}\cdot\vec{d} = F_{x}d_{x} + F_{y}d_{y} | = (-500000)(866025.4) + (866025.4)(500000) = 0 | Magnitudes: F = 1000000,\quad d = 1000000 | Angle between them: \cos\theta = \frac{\vec{F}\cdot\vec{d}}{F\,d} = \frac{0}{1000000\times1000000} = 0 | \theta = \cos^{-1}(0) = 90^\circ | The dot product is zero, so the vectors are perpendicular (θ = 90°). | W = 0\,\text{J}  =>  W = 0\,\text{J}, θ = 90^\circ

op relvel [-500000,866025.40378,0] [866025.40378,500000,0]  =>  [Velocity of A relative to B] Subtracting a vector means adding its negative (same magnitude, opposite direction): \vec{v_{A}} - \vec{v_{B}} = \vec{v_{A}} + (-\vec{v_{B}}) | -\vec{v_{B}} = -866025.4\hat{i} - 500000\hat{j} | Add the x-components together, the y-components together. | v_{AB,x} = v_{A,x} + (-v_{B})_{x} = (-500000) + (-866025.4) = -1366025.4 | v_{AB,y} = v_{A,y} + (-v_{B})_{y} = 866025.4 + (-500000) = 366025.4 | Resultant vector: \vec{v_{AB}} = -1366025.4\hat{i} + 366025.4\hat{j} | Magnitude: v_{AB} = \sqrt{v_{AB,x}^2 + v_{AB,y}^2} = \sqrt{(-1366025.4)^2 + 366025.4^2} = \sqrt{2\times 10^{12}} = 1414213.56 | Direction. First find v × B. | Given: \vec{v} = \left(1\hat{i} + 2\hat{j} + 2\hat{k}\right)\times 10^{-19},\quad \vec{B} = \vec{0} | Write the cross product as a determinant: \vec{v}\times\vec{B} = \begin{vmatrix} \hat{i} & \hat{j} & \hat{k} \\ 1\times 10^{-19} & 2\times 10^{-19} & 2\times 10^{-19} \\ 0 & 0 & 0 \end{vmatrix} | Expand along the first row: = \hat{i}\,[(2\times 10^{-19})(0) - (2\times 10^{-19})(0)] - \hat{j}\,[(1\times 10^{-19})(0) - (2\times 10^{-19})(0)] + \hat{k}\,[(1\times 10^{-19})(0) - (2\times 10^{-19})(0)] | \vec{v×B} = \vec{0} | Magnitude: \left|\vec{v×B}\right| = \sqrt{0^2 + 0^2 + 0^2} = \sqrt{0} = 0 | Multiply by the charge q = 1.6 × 10⁻¹⁹ C: \vec{F} = 1.6\times 10^{-19}\,(\vec{0}) = \vec{0}\,\text{N} | \left|\vec{F}\right| = 0\,\text{N}  =>  F = \vec{0}\,\text{N}, |F| = 0\,\text{N}
op relvel [1e-19,2e-19,2e-19] [0,0,0]  =>  [Velocity of A relative to B] Subtracting a vector means adding its negative (same magnitude, opposite direction): \vec{v_{A}} - \vec{v_{B}} = \vec{v_{A}} + (-\vec{v_{B}}) | -\vec{v_{B}} = \vec{0} | Add the x-components together, the y-components together and the z-components together. | v_{AB,x} = v_{A,x} + (-v_{B})_{x} = 1\times 10^{-19} + 0 = 1\times 10^{-19} | v_{AB,y} = v_{A,y} + (-v_{B})_{y} = 2\times 10^{-19} + 0 = 2\times 10^{-19} | v_{AB,z} = v_{A,z} + (-v_{B})_{z} = 2\times 10^{-19} + 0 = 2\times 10^{-19} | Resultant vector: \vec{v_{AB}} = \left(1\hat{i} + 2\hat{j} + 2\hat{k}\right)\times 10^{-19} | Magnitude (Pythagorean theorem in 3D): v_{AB} = \sqrt{v_{AB,x}^2 + v_{AB,y}^2 + v_{AB,z}^2} = \sqrt{(1\times 10^{-19})^2 + (2\times 10^{-19})^2 + (2\times 10^{-19})^2} = \sqrt{9\times 10^{-38}} = 3\times 10^{-19} | Angle with the x-axis (direction cosine): \alpha = \cos^{-1}\left(\frac{v_{AB,x}}{v_{AB}}\right) = \cos^{-1}\left(\frac{1\times 10^{-19}}{3\times 10^{-19}}\right) = 70.53^\circ | Angle with the y-axis (direction cosine): \beta = \cos^{-1}\left(\frac{v_{AB,y}}{v_{AB}}\right) = \cos^{-1}\left(\frac{2\times 10^{-19}}{3\times 10^{-19}}\right) = 48.19^\circ | Angle with the z-axis (direction cosine): \gamma = \cos^{-1}\left(\frac{v_{AB,z}}{v_{AB}}\right) = \cos^{-1}\left(\frac{2\times 10^{-19}}{3\times 10^{-19}}\right) = 48.19^\circ  =>  v_AB = \left(1\hat{i} + 2\hat{j} + 2\hat{k}\right)\times 10^{-19}, |v_AB| = 3\times 10^{-19}, α, β, γ = 70.53^\circ,\ 48.19^\circ,\ 48.19^\circ

op sum [1e-7,3e-7,0] [2e-7,-1e-7,0]  =>  [Resultant R = A + B] Write every vector in component form: \vec{A} = 1\times 10^{-7}\hat{i} + 3\times 10^{-7}\hat{j},\quad \vec{B} = 2\times 10^{-7}\hat{i} - 1\times 10^{-7}\hat{j} | Add the x-components together, the y-components together. | R_{x} = A_{x} + B_{x} = (1\times 10^{-7}) + (2\times 10^{-7}) = 3\times 10^{-7} | R_{y} = A_{y} + B_{y} = (3\times 10^{-7}) + (-1\times 10^{-7}) = 2\times 10^{-7} | Resultant vector: \vec{R} = 3\times 10^{-7}\hat{i} + 2\times 10^{-7}\hat{j} | Magnitude: R = \sqrt{R_{x}^2 + R_{y}^2} = \sqrt{(3\times 10^{-7})^2 + (2\times 10^{-7})^2} = \sqrt{1.3\times 10^{-13}} = 3.61\times 10^{-7} | Direction. First the reference angle, ignoring the signs: \theta_{ref} = \tan^{-1}\left(\frac{|R_{y}|}{|R_{x}|}\right) = \tan^{-1}\left(\frac{2\times 10^{-7}}{3\times 10^{-7}}\right) = 33.69^\circ | Rx is + and Ry is +, so the vector is in the 1st quadrant. \theta = \theta_{ref} = 33.69^\circ  =>  R = 3\times 10^{-7}\hat{i} + 2\times 10^{-7}\hat{j}, |R| = 3.61\times 10^{-7}, θ = 33.69^\circ
op sub [1e-7,3e-7,0] [2e-7,-1e-7,0]  =>  [R = A − B] Subtracting a vector means adding its negative (same magnitude, opposite direction): \vec{A} - \vec{B} = \vec{A} + (-\vec{B}) | -\vec{B} = -2\times 10^{-7}\hat{i} + 1\times 10^{-7}\hat{j} | Add the x-components together, the y-components together. | R_{x} = A_{x} + (-B)_{x} = (1\times 10^{-7}) + (-2\times 10^{-7}) = -1\times 10^{-7} | R_{y} = A_{y} + (-B)_{y} = (3\times 10^{-7}) + (1\times 10^{-7}) = 4\times 10^{-7} | Resultant vector: \vec{R} = -1\times 10^{-7}\hat{i} + 4\times 10^{-7}\hat{j} | Magnitude: R = \sqrt{R_{x}^2 + R_{y}^2} = \sqrt{(-1\times 10^{-7})^2 + (4\times 10^{-7})^2} = \sqrt{1.7\times 10^{-13}} = 4.12\times 10^{-7} | Direction. First the reference angle, ignoring the signs: \theta_{ref} = \tan^{-1}\left(\frac{|R_{y}|}{|R_{x}|}\right) = \tan^{-1}\left(\frac{4\times 10^{-7}}{1\times 10^{-7}}\right) = 75.96^\circ | Rx is − and Ry is +, so the vector is in the 2nd quadrant. \theta = 180^\circ - \theta_{ref} = 180^\circ - 75.96^\circ = 104.04^\circ  =>  R = -1\times 10^{-7}\hat{i} + 4\times 10^{-7}\hat{j}, |R| = 4.12\times 10^{-7}, θ = 104.04^\circ
op dot [1e-7,3e-7,0] [2e-7,-1e-7,0]  =>  [Scalar product A·B] Given: \vec{A} = 1\times 10^{-7}\hat{i} + 3\times 10^{-7}\hat{j},\quad \vec{B} = 2\times 10^{-7}\hat{i} - 1\times 10^{-7}\hat{j} | Scalar product in terms of rectangular components: \vec{A}\cdot\vec{B} = A_{x}B_{x} + A_{y}B_{y} | = (1\times 10^{-7})(2\times 10^{-7}) + (3\times 10^{-7})(-1\times 10^{-7}) = -1\times 10^{-14} | Magnitudes: A = 3.16\times 10^{-7},\quad B = 2.24\times 10^{-7} | Angle between them: \cos\theta = \frac{\vec{A}\cdot\vec{B}}{A\,B} = \frac{-1\times 10^{-14}}{3.162\times 10^{-7}\times2.236\times 10^{-7}} = -0.1414 | \theta = \cos^{-1}(-0.1414) = 98.13^\circ | Meaning: A·B = |A| × (projection of B on A). B\cos\theta = 2.236\times 10^{-7}\cos 98.13^\circ = -3.16\times 10^{-8}  =>  A·B = -1\times 10^{-14}, θ = 98.13^\circ
op cross [1e-7,3e-7,0] [2e-7,-1e-7,0]  =>  [Vector product A×B] Given: \vec{A} = 1\times 10^{-7}\hat{i} + 3\times 10^{-7}\hat{j},\quad \vec{B} = 2\times 10^{-7}\hat{i} - 1\times 10^{-7}\hat{j} | Write the cross product as a determinant: \vec{A}\times\vec{B} = \begin{vmatrix} \hat{i} & \hat{j} & \hat{k} \\ 1\times 10^{-7} & 3\times 10^{-7} & 0 \\ 2\times 10^{-7} & -1\times 10^{-7} & 0 \end{vmatrix} | Expand along the first row: = \hat{i}\,[(3\times 10^{-7})(0) - (0)(-1\times 10^{-7})] - \hat{j}\,[(1\times 10^{-7})(0) - (0)(2\times 10^{-7})] + \hat{k}\,[(1\times 10^{-7})(-1\times 10^{-7}) - (3\times 10^{-7})(2\times 10^{-7})] | \vec{C} = \left(-7\hat{k}\right)\times 10^{-14} | Magnitude: \left|\vec{C}\right| = \sqrt{0^2 + 0^2 + (-7\times 10^{-14})^2} = \sqrt{4.9\times 10^{-27}} = 7\times 10^{-14} | Check with |A×B| = AB sin θ: 3.162\times 10^{-7}\times2.236\times 10^{-7}\times\sin 98.13^\circ = 7\times 10^{-14} | |A×B| is also the area of the parallelogram with sides A and B. \text{Area} = 7\times 10^{-14} | The direction is perpendicular to the plane of A and B (right-hand rule). The order matters: \vec{B}\times\vec{A} = -\vec{A}\times\vec{B} = \left(7\hat{k}\right)\times 10^{-14}  =>  A×B = \left(-7\hat{k}\right)\times 10^{-14}, |A×B| = 7\times 10^{-14}, θ = 98.13^\circ

op torque [1e-7,3e-7,0] [2e-7,-1e-7,0]  =>  [Torque τ = r × F] Torque is the vector product of the position vector r and the force F. \vec{\tau} = \vec{r}\times\vec{F} | Given: \vec{r} = 1\times 10^{-7}\hat{i} + 3\times 10^{-7}\hat{j},\quad \vec{F} = 2\times 10^{-7}\hat{i} - 1\times 10^{-7}\hat{j} | Write the cross product as a determinant: \vec{r}\times\vec{F} = \begin{vmatrix} \hat{i} & \hat{j} & \hat{k} \\ 1\times 10^{-7} & 3\times 10^{-7} & 0 \\ 2\times 10^{-7} & -1\times 10^{-7} & 0 \end{vmatrix} | Expand along the first row: = \hat{i}\,[(3\times 10^{-7})(0) - (0)(-1\times 10^{-7})] - \hat{j}\,[(1\times 10^{-7})(0) - (0)(2\times 10^{-7})] + \hat{k}\,[(1\times 10^{-7})(-1\times 10^{-7}) - (3\times 10^{-7})(2\times 10^{-7})] | \vec{τ} = \left(-7\hat{k}\right)\times 10^{-14} | Magnitude: \left|\vec{τ}\right| = \sqrt{0^2 + 0^2 + (-7\times 10^{-14})^2} = \sqrt{4.9\times 10^{-27}} = 7\times 10^{-14} | Check with |r×F| = rF sin θ: 3.162\times 10^{-7}\times2.236\times 10^{-7}\times\sin 98.13^\circ = 7\times 10^{-14} | |r×F| is also the area of the parallelogram with sides r and F. \text{Area} = 7\times 10^{-14} | The direction is perpendicular to the plane of r and F (right-hand rule). op cross [30000000000,40000000000,0] [-20000000000,50000000000,0]  =>  [Vector product A×B] Given: \vec{A} = 3\times 10^{10}\hat{i} + 4\times 10^{10}\hat{j},\quad \vec{B} = -2\times 10^{10}\hat{i} + 5\times 10^{10}\hat{j} | Write the cross product as a determinant: \vec{A}\times\vec{B} = \begin{vmatrix} \hat{i} & \hat{j} & \hat{k} \\ 3\times 10^{10} & 4\times 10^{10} & 0 \\ -2\times 10^{10} & 5\times 10^{10} & 0 \end{vmatrix} | Expand along the first row: = \hat{i}\,[(4\times 10^{10})(0) - (0)(5\times 10^{10})] - \hat{j}\,[(3\times 10^{10})(0) - (0)(-2\times 10^{10})] + \hat{k}\,[(3\times 10^{10})(5\times 10^{10}) - (4\times 10^{10})(-2\times 10^{10})] | \vec{C} = 2.3\times 10^{21}\hat{k} | Magnitude: \left|\vec{C}\right| = \sqrt{0^2 + 0^2 + (2.3\times 10^{21})^2} = \sqrt{5.29\times 10^{42}} = 2.3\times 10^{21} | Check with |A×B| = AB sin θ: 5\times 10^{10}\times5.39\times 10^{10}\times\sin 58.67^\circ = 2.3\times 10^{21} | |A×B| is also the area of the parallelogram with sides A and B. \text{Area} = 2.3\times 10^{21} | The direction is perpendicular to the plane of A and B (right-hand rule).
