op cross [1e-7,3e-7,2e-7] [2e-7,-1e-7,1e-7]  =>  [Vector product A×B] Given: \vec{A} = 1\times 10^{-7}\hat{i} + 3\times 10^{-7}\hat{j} + 2\times 10^{-7}\hat{k},\quad \vec{B} = 2\times 10^{-7}\hat{i} - 1\times 10^{-7}\hat{j} + 1\times 10^{-7}\hat{k} | Write the cross product as a determinant: \vec{A}\times\vec{B} = \begin{vmatrix} \hat{i} & \hat{j} & \hat{k} \\ 1\times 10^{-7} & 3\times 10^{-7} & 2\times 10^{-7} \\ 2\times 10^{-7} & -1\times 10^{-7} & 1\times 10^{-7} \end{vmatrix} | Expand along the first row: = \hat{i}\,[(3\times 10^{-7})(1\times 10^{-7}) - (2\times 10^{-7})(-1\times 10^{-7})] - \hat{j}\,[(1\times 10^{-7})(1\times 10^{-7}) - (2\times 10^{-7})(2\times 10^{-7})] + \hat{k}\,[(1\times 10^{-7})(-1\times 10^{-7}) - (3\times 10^{-7})(2\times 10^{-7})] | \vec{C} = \left(5\hat{i} + 3\hat{j} - 7\hat{k}\right)\times 10^{-14} | Magnitude: \left|\vec{C}\right| = \sqrt{(5\times 10^{-14})^2 + (3\times 10^{-14})^2 + (-7\times 10^{-14})^2} = \sqrt{8.3\times 10^{-27}} = 9.11\times 10^{-14} | Check with |A×B| = AB sin θ: 3.74\times 10^{-7}\times2.45\times 10^{-7}\times\sin 83.74^\circ = 9.11\times 10^{-14} | |A×B| is also the area of the parallelogram with sides A and B. \text{Area} = 9.11\times 10^{-14} | The direction is perpendicular to the plane of A and B (right-hand rule). The order matters: \vec{B}\times\vec{A} = -\vec{A}\times\vec{B} = \left(-5\hat{i} - 3\hat{j} + 7\hat{k}\right)\times 10^{-14}  =>  A×B = \left(5\hat{i} + 3\hat{j} - 7\hat{k}\right)\times 10^{-14}, |A×B| = 9.11\times 10^{-14}, θ = 83.74^\circ
op mag [1e-7,3e-7,2e-7] [2e-7,-1e-7,1e-7]  =>  [Magnitude and direction of A] Given components: \vec{A} = 1\times 10^{-7}\hat{i} + 3\times 10^{-7}\hat{j} + 2\times 10^{-7}\hat{k} | Magnitude (Pythagorean theorem in 3D): A = \sqrt{A_{x}^2 + A_{y}^2 + A_{z}^2} = \sqrt{(1\times 10^{-7})^2 + (3\times 10^{-7})^2 + (2\times 10^{-7})^2} = \sqrt{1.4\times 10^{-13}} = 3.74\times 10^{-7} | Angle with the x-axis (direction cosine): \alpha = \cos^{-1}\left(\frac{A_{x}}{A}\right) = \cos^{-1}\left(\frac{1\times 10^{-7}}{3.742\times 10^{-7}}\right) = 74.5^\circ | Angle with the y-axis (direction cosine): \beta = \cos^{-1}\left(\frac{A_{y}}{A}\right) = \cos^{-1}\left(\frac{3\times 10^{-7}}{3.7417\times 10^{-7}}\right) = 36.7^\circ | Angle with the z-axis (direction cosine): \gamma = \cos^{-1}\left(\frac{A_{z}}{A}\right) = \cos^{-1}\left(\frac{2\times 10^{-7}}{3.742\times 10^{-7}}\right) = 57.69^\circ  =>  |A| = 3.74\times 10^{-7}, α, β, γ = 74.5^\circ,\ 36.7^\circ,\ 57.69^\circ
op resolve [1e-7,3e-7,2e-7] [2e-7,-1e-7,1e-7]  =>  [Components of A] Given components: \vec{A} = 1\times 10^{-7}\hat{i} + 3\times 10^{-7}\hat{j} + 2\times 10^{-7}\hat{k} | Read each component off the unit-vector form: A_{x} = 1\times 10^{-7},\quad A_{y} = 3\times 10^{-7},\quad A_{z} = 2\times 10^{-7} | Magnitude (Pythagorean theorem in 3D): A = \sqrt{A_{x}^2 + A_{y}^2 + A_{z}^2} = \sqrt{(1\times 10^{-7})^2 + (3\times 10^{-7})^2 + (2\times 10^{-7})^2} = \sqrt{1.4\times 10^{-13}} = 3.74\times 10^{-7} | Angle with the x-axis (direction cosine): \alpha = \cos^{-1}\left(\frac{A_{x}}{A}\right) = \cos^{-1}\left(\frac{1\times 10^{-7}}{3.742\times 10^{-7}}\right) = 74.5^\circ | Angle with the y-axis (direction cosine): \beta = \cos^{-1}\left(\frac{A_{y}}{A}\right) = \cos^{-1}\left(\frac{3\times 10^{-7}}{3.7417\times 10^{-7}}\right) = 36.7^\circ | Angle with the z-axis (direction cosine): \gamma = \cos^{-1}\left(\frac{A_{z}}{A}\right) = \cos^{-1}\left(\frac{2\times 10^{-7}}{3.742\times 10^{-7}}\right) = 57.69^\circ  =>  Ax = 1\times 10^{-7}, Ay = 3\times 10^{-7}, Az = 2\times 10^{-7}, |A| = 3.74\times 10^{-7}, α, β, γ = 74.5^\circ,\ 36.7^\circ,\ 57.69^\circ
op scale [1e-7,3e-7,2e-7] [2e-7,-1e-7,1e-7]  =>  [R = 2A] Multiply every component by the scalar: 2\,\vec{A} = 2(1\times 10^{-7}\hat{i} + 3\times 10^{-7}\hat{j} + 2\times 10^{-7}\hat{k}) = 2\times 10^{-7}\hat{i} + 6\times 10^{-7}\hat{j} + 4\times 10^{-7}\hat{k} | The magnitude is multiplied by |k|: \left|2\,\vec{A}\right| = |2|\times 3.74\times 10^{-7} = 7.48\times 10^{-7} | k is positive, so the direction does not change.  =>  R = 2\times 10^{-7}\hat{i} + 6\times 10^{-7}\hat{j} + 4\times 10^{-7}\hat{k}, |R| = 7.48\times 10^{-7}
op unit [1e-7,3e-7,2e-7] [2e-7,-1e-7,1e-7]  =>  [Unit vector along A] A unit vector has magnitude 1 and points the same way as the vector. \hat{a} = \frac{\vec{A}}{A} | A = 3.74\times 10^{-7} | \hat{a} = \frac{1\times 10^{-7}\hat{i} + 3\times 10^{-7}\hat{j} + 2\times 10^{-7}\hat{k}}{3.74\times 10^{-7}} = 0.27\hat{i} + 0.8\hat{j} + 0.53\hat{k} | Check: its magnitude is \sqrt{0.27^2 + 0.8^2 + 0.53^2} = 1  =>  unit vector = 0.27\hat{i} + 0.8\hat{j} + 0.53\hat{k}
op angle [1e-7,3e-7,2e-7] [2e-7,-1e-7,1e-7]  =>  [Angle between A and B] Given: \vec{A} = 1\times 10^{-7}\hat{i} + 3\times 10^{-7}\hat{j} + 2\times 10^{-7}\hat{k},\quad \vec{B} = 2\times 10^{-7}\hat{i} - 1\times 10^{-7}\hat{j} + 1\times 10^{-7}\hat{k} | Scalar product in terms of rectangular components: \vec{A}\cdot\vec{B} = A_{x}B_{x} + A_{y}B_{y} + A_{z}B_{z} | = (1\times 10^{-7})(2\times 10^{-7}) + (3\times 10^{-7})(-1\times 10^{-7}) + (2\times 10^{-7})(1\times 10^{-7}) = 1\times 10^{-14} | Magnitudes: A = 3.74\times 10^{-7},\quad B = 2.45\times 10^{-7} | Angle between them: \cos\theta = \frac{\vec{A}\cdot\vec{B}}{A\,B} = \frac{1\times 10^{-14}}{3.74\times 10^{-7}\times2.45\times 10^{-7}} = 0.109 | \theta = \cos^{-1}(0.109) = 83.74^\circ  =>  θ = 83.74^\circ
op proj [1e-7,3e-7,2e-7] [2e-7,-1e-7,1e-7]  =>  [Projection of B on A] Scalar projection of B on A (B cos θ): B\cos\theta = \frac{\vec{A}\cdot\vec{B}}{A} = \frac{1\times 10^{-14}}{3.74\times 10^{-7}} = 2.67\times 10^{-8} | Vector projection (the shadow of B along A): \text{proj}_{A}\vec{B} = \frac{\vec{A}\cdot\vec{B}}{A^2}\,\vec{A} = \frac{1\times 10^{-14}}{1.4\times 10^{-13}}(1\times 10^{-7}\hat{i} + 3\times 10^{-7}\hat{j} + 2\times 10^{-7}\hat{k}) = 7.14\times 10^{-9}\hat{i} + 2.14\times 10^{-8}\hat{j} + 1.43\times 10^{-8}\hat{k}  =>  B cos θ = 2.67\times 10^{-8}, proj of B on A = 7.14\times 10^{-9}\hat{i} + 2.14\times 10^{-8}\hat{j} + 1.43\times 10^{-8}\hat{k}
op equil [1e-7,3e-7,2e-7] [2e-7,-1e-7,1e-7]  =>  [Force needed for equilibrium] Write every vector in component form: \vec{A} = 1\times 10^{-7}\hat{i} + 3\times 10^{-7}\hat{j} + 2\times 10^{-7}\hat{k},\quad \vec{B} = 2\times 10^{-7}\hat{i} - 1\times 10^{-7}\hat{j} + 1\times 10^{-7}\hat{k} | Add the x-components together, the y-components together and the z-components together. | R_{x} = A_{x} + B_{x} = (1\times 10^{-7}) + (2\times 10^{-7}) = 3\times 10^{-7} | R_{y} = A_{y} + B_{y} = (3\times 10^{-7}) + (-1\times 10^{-7}) = 2\times 10^{-7} | R_{z} = A_{z} + B_{z} = (2\times 10^{-7}) + (1\times 10^{-7}) = 3\times 10^{-7} | Resultant vector: \vec{R} = 3\times 10^{-7}\hat{i} + 2\times 10^{-7}\hat{j} + 3\times 10^{-7}\hat{k} | Magnitude (Pythagorean theorem in 3D): R = \sqrt{R_{x}^2 + R_{y}^2 + R_{z}^2} = \sqrt{(3\times 10^{-7})^2 + (2\times 10^{-7})^2 + (3\times 10^{-7})^2} = \sqrt{2.2\times 10^{-13}} = 4.69\times 10^{-7} | Angle with the x-axis (direction cosine): \alpha = \cos^{-1}\left(\frac{R_{x}}{R}\right) = \cos^{-1}\left(\frac{3\times 10^{-7}}{4.6904\times 10^{-7}}\right) = 50.24^\circ | Angle with the y-axis (direction cosine): \beta = \cos^{-1}\left(\frac{R_{y}}{R}\right) = \cos^{-1}\left(\frac{2\times 10^{-7}}{4.69\times 10^{-7}}\right) = 64.76^\circ | Angle with the z-axis (direction cosine): \gamma = \cos^{-1}\left(\frac{R_{z}}{R}\right) = \cos^{-1}\left(\frac{3\times 10^{-7}}{4.6904\times 10^{-7}}\right) = 50.24^\circ | For equilibrium the net force must be zero, so the balancing force (equilibrant) is equal and opposite to R: \vec{E} = -\vec{R} = -3\times 10^{-7}\hat{i} - 2\times 10^{-7}\hat{j} - 3\times 10^{-7}\hat{k} | \left|\vec{E}\right| = 4.69\times 10^{-7}  =>  E = -3\times 10^{-7}\hat{i} - 2\times 10^{-7}\hat{j} - 3\times 10^{-7}\hat{k}, |E| = 4.69\times 10^{-7}
op torque [1e-7,3e-7,2e-7] [2e-7,-1e-7,1e-7]  =>  [Torque τ = r × F] Torque is the vector product of the position vector r and the force F. \vec{\tau} = \vec{r}\times\vec{F} | Given: \vec{r} = 1\times 10^{-7}\hat{i} + 3\times 10^{-7}\hat{j} + 2\times 10^{-7}\hat{k},\quad \vec{F} = 2\times 10^{-7}\hat{i} - 1\times 10^{-7}\hat{j} + 1\times 10^{-7}\hat{k} | Write the cross product as a determinant: \vec{r}\times\vec{F} = \begin{vmatrix} \hat{i} & \hat{j} & \hat{k} \\ 1\times 10^{-7} & 3\times 10^{-7} & 2\times 10^{-7} \\ 2\times 10^{-7} & -1\times 10^{-7} & 1\times 10^{-7} \end{vmatrix} | Expand along the first row: = \hat{i}\,[(3\times 10^{-7})(1\times 10^{-7}) - (2\times 10^{-7})(-1\times 10^{-7})] - \hat{j}\,[(1\times 10^{-7})(1\times 10^{-7}) - (2\times 10^{-7})(2\times 10^{-7})] + \hat{k}\,[(1\times 10^{-7})(-1\times 10^{-7}) - (3\times 10^{-7})(2\times 10^{-7})] | \vec{τ} = \left(5\hat{i} + 3\hat{j} - 7\hat{k}\right)\times 10^{-14} | Magnitude: \left|\vec{τ}\right| = \sqrt{(5\times 10^{-14})^2 + (3\times 10^{-14})^2 + (-7\times 10^{-14})^2} = \sqrt{8.3\times 10^{-27}} = 9.11\times 10^{-14} | Check with |r×F| = rF sin θ: 3.74\times 10^{-7}\times2.45\times 10^{-7}\times\sin 83.74^\circ = 9.11\times 10^{-14} | |r×F| is also the area of the parallelogram with sides r and F. \text{Area} = 9.11\times 10^{-14} | The direction is perpendicular to the plane of r and F (right-hand rule). The order matters: \vec{F}\times\vec{r} = -\vec{r}\times\vec{F} = \left(-5\hat{i} - 3\hat{j} + 7\hat{k}\right)\times 10^{-14}  =>  τ = \left(5\hat{i} + 3\hat{j} - 7\hat{k}\right)\times 10^{-14}\,\text{N m}, |τ| = 9.11\times 10^{-14}\,\text{N m}, θ = 83.74^\circ
op work [1e-7,3e-7,2e-7] [2e-7,-1e-7,1e-7]  =>  [Work done W = F · d] Work is the scalar product of force and displacement: W = F·d = Fd cos θ. | Given: \vec{F} = 1\times 10^{-7}\hat{i} + 3\times 10^{-7}\hat{j} + 2\times 10^{-7}\hat{k},\quad \vec{d} = 2\times 10^{-7}\hat{i} - 1\times 10^{-7}\hat{j} + 1\times 10^{-7}\hat{k} | Scalar product in terms of rectangular components: \vec{F}\cdot\vec{d} = F_{x}d_{x} + F_{y}d_{y} + F_{z}d_{z} | = (1\times 10^{-7})(2\times 10^{-7}) + (3\times 10^{-7})(-1\times 10^{-7}) + (2\times 10^{-7})(1\times 10^{-7}) = 1\times 10^{-14} | Magnitudes: F = 3.74\times 10^{-7},\quad d = 2.45\times 10^{-7} | Angle between them: \cos\theta = \frac{\vec{F}\cdot\vec{d}}{F\,d} = \frac{1\times 10^{-14}}{3.74\times 10^{-7}\times2.45\times 10^{-7}} = 0.109 | \theta = \cos^{-1}(0.109) = 83.74^\circ | W = 1\times 10^{-14}\,\text{J}  =>  W = 1\times 10^{-14}\,\text{J}, θ = 83.74^\circ
op lorentz [1e-7,3e-7,2e-7] [2e-7,-1e-7,1e-7]  =>  [Magnetic force F = q(v × B)] The force on a moving charge is F = q(v × B). First find v × B. | Given: \vec{v} = 1\times 10^{-7}\hat{i} + 3\times 10^{-7}\hat{j} + 2\times 10^{-7}\hat{k},\quad \vec{B} = 2\times 10^{-7}\hat{i} - 1\times 10^{-7}\hat{j} + 1\times 10^{-7}\hat{k} | Write the cross product as a determinant: \vec{v}\times\vec{B} = \begin{vmatrix} \hat{i} & \hat{j} & \hat{k} \\ 1\times 10^{-7} & 3\times 10^{-7} & 2\times 10^{-7} \\ 2\times 10^{-7} & -1\times 10^{-7} & 1\times 10^{-7} \end{vmatrix} | Expand along the first row: = \hat{i}\,[(3\times 10^{-7})(1\times 10^{-7}) - (2\times 10^{-7})(-1\times 10^{-7})] - \hat{j}\,[(1\times 10^{-7})(1\times 10^{-7}) - (2\times 10^{-7})(2\times 10^{-7})] + \hat{k}\,[(1\times 10^{-7})(-1\times 10^{-7}) - (3\times 10^{-7})(2\times 10^{-7})] | \vec{v×B} = \left(5\hat{i} + 3\hat{j} - 7\hat{k}\right)\times 10^{-14} | Magnitude: \left|\vec{v×B}\right| = \sqrt{(5\times 10^{-14})^2 + (3\times 10^{-14})^2 + (-7\times 10^{-14})^2} = \sqrt{8.3\times 10^{-27}} = 9.11\times 10^{-14} | Multiply by the charge q = 1.6 × 10⁻¹⁹ C: \vec{F} = 1.6\times 10^{-19}\,(\left(5\hat{i} + 3\hat{j} - 7\hat{k}\right)\times 10^{-14}) = \left(0.8\hat{i} + 0.48\hat{j} - 1.12\hat{k}\right)\times 10^{-32}\,\text{N} | \left|\vec{F}\right| = 1.46\times 10^{-32}\,\text{N}  =>  F = \left(0.8\hat{i} + 0.48\hat{j} - 1.12\hat{k}\right)\times 10^{-32}\,\text{N}, |F| = 1.46\times 10^{-32}\,\text{N}
op relvel [1e-7,3e-7,2e-7] [2e-7,-1e-7,1e-7]  =>  [Velocity of A relative to B] Subtracting a vector means adding its negative (same magnitude, opposite direction): \vec{v_{A}} - \vec{v_{B}} = \vec{v_{A}} + (-\vec{v_{B}}) | -\vec{v_{B}} = -2\times 10^{-7}\hat{i} + 1\times 10^{-7}\hat{j} - 1\times 10^{-7}\hat{k} | Add the x-components together, the y-components together and the z-components together. | v_{AB,x} = v_{A,x} + (-v_{B})_{x} = (1\times 10^{-7}) + (-2\times 10^{-7}) = -1\times 10^{-7} | v_{AB,y} = v_{A,y} + (-v_{B})_{y} = (3\times 10^{-7}) + (1\times 10^{-7}) = 4\times 10^{-7} | v_{AB,z} = v_{A,z} + (-v_{B})_{z} = (2\times 10^{-7}) + (-1\times 10^{-7}) = 1\times 10^{-7} | Resultant vector: \vec{v_{AB}} = -1\times 10^{-7}\hat{i} + 4\times 10^{-7}\hat{j} + 1\times 10^{-7}\hat{k} | Magnitude (Pythagorean theorem in 3D): v_{AB} = \sqrt{v_{AB,x}^2 + v_{AB,y}^2 + v_{AB,z}^2} = \sqrt{(-1\times 10^{-7})^2 + (4\times 10^{-7})^2 + (1\times 10^{-7})^2} = \sqrt{1.8\times 10^{-13}} = 4.24\times 10^{-7} | Angle with the x-axis (direction cosine): \alpha = \cos^{-1}\left(\frac{v_{AB,x}}{v_{AB}}\right) = \cos^{-1}\left(\frac{-1\times 10^{-7}}{4.243\times 10^{-7}}\right) = 103.63^\circ | Angle with the y-axis (direction cosine): \beta = \cos^{-1}\left(\frac{v_{AB,y}}{v_{AB}}\right) = \cos^{-1}\left(\frac{4\times 10^{-7}}{4.2426\times 10^{-7}}\right) = 19.47^\circ | Angle with the z-axis (direction cosine): \gamma = \cos^{-1}\left(\frac{v_{AB,z}}{v_{AB}}\right) = \cos^{-1}\left(\frac{1\times 10^{-7}}{4.243\times 10^{-7}}\right) = 76.37^\circ  =>  v_AB = -1\times 10^{-7}\hat{i} + 4\times 10^{-7}\hat{j} + 1\times 10^{-7}\hat{k}, |v_AB| = 4.24\times 10^{-7}, α, β, γ = 103.63^\circ,\ 19.47^\circ,\ 76.37^\circ
cardLineTex [4,0,0]  =>  \vec{A} = 4\hat{i}\quad |A| = 4\,\text{u}\quad \theta = 0^\circ
op sum [4,0,0] [0,4,0]  =>  [Resultant R = A + B] Write every vector in component form: \vec{A} = 4\hat{i},\quad \vec{B} = 4\hat{j} | Add the x-components together, the y-components together. | R_{x} = A_{x} + B_{x} = 4 + 0 = 4 | R_{y} = A_{y} + B_{y} = 0 + 4 = 4 | Resultant vector: \vec{R} = 4\hat{i} + 4\hat{j} | Magnitude: R = \sqrt{R_{x}^2 + R_{y}^2} = \sqrt{4^2 + 4^2} = \sqrt{32} = 5.66 | Direction. First the reference angle, ignoring the signs: \theta_{ref} = \tan^{-1}\left(\frac{|R_{y}|}{|R_{x}|}\right) = \tan^{-1}\left(\frac{4}{4}\right) = 45^\circ | Rx is + and Ry is +, so the vector is in the 1st quadrant. \theta = \theta_{ref} = 45^\circ  =>  R = 4\hat{i} + 4\hat{j}, |R| = 5.66, θ = 45^\circ
op sub [4,0,0] [0,4,0]  =>  [R = A − B] Subtracting a vector means adding its negative (same magnitude, opposite direction): \vec{A} - \vec{B} = \vec{A} + (-\vec{B}) | -\vec{B} = -4\hat{j} | Add the x-components together, the y-components together. | R_{x} = A_{x} + (-B)_{x} = 4 + 0 = 4 | R_{y} = A_{y} + (-B)_{y} = 0 + (-4) = -4 | Resultant vector: \vec{R} = 4\hat{i} - 4\hat{j} | Magnitude: R = \sqrt{R_{x}^2 + R_{y}^2} = \sqrt{4^2 + (-4)^2} = \sqrt{32} = 5.66 | Direction. First the reference angle, ignoring the signs: \theta_{ref} = \tan^{-1}\left(\frac{|R_{y}|}{|R_{x}|}\right) = \tan^{-1}\left(\frac{4}{4}\right) = 45^\circ | Rx is + and Ry is −, so the vector is in the 4th quadrant. \theta = 360^\circ - \theta_{ref} = 360^\circ - 45^\circ = 315^\circ  =>  R = 4\hat{i} - 4\hat{j}, |R| = 5.66, θ = 315^\circ
op dot [4,0,0] [0,4,0]  =>  [Scalar product A·B] Given: \vec{A} = 4\hat{i},\quad \vec{B} = 4\hat{j} | Scalar product in terms of rectangular components: \vec{A}\cdot\vec{B} = A_{x}B_{x} + A_{y}B_{y} | = (4)(0) + (0)(4) = 0 | Magnitudes: A = 4,\quad B = 4 | Angle between them: \cos\theta = \frac{\vec{A}\cdot\vec{B}}{A\,B} = \frac{0}{4\times4} = 0 | \theta = \cos^{-1}(0) = 90^\circ | The dot product is zero, so the vectors are perpendicular (θ = 90°). | Meaning: A·B = |A| × (projection of B on A). B\cos\theta = 4\cos 90^\circ = 0  =>  A·B = 0, θ = 90^\circ
op cross [4,0,0] [0,4,0]  =>  [Vector product A×B] Given: \vec{A} = 4\hat{i},\quad \vec{B} = 4\hat{j} | Write the cross product as a determinant: \vec{A}\times\vec{B} = \begin{vmatrix} \hat{i} & \hat{j} & \hat{k} \\ 4 & 0 & 0 \\ 0 & 4 & 0 \end{vmatrix} | Expand along the first row: = \hat{i}\,[(0)(0) - (0)(4)] - \hat{j}\,[(4)(0) - (0)(0)] + \hat{k}\,[(4)(4) - (0)(0)] | \vec{C} = 16\hat{k} | Magnitude: \left|\vec{C}\right| = \sqrt{0^2 + 0^2 + 16^2} = \sqrt{256} = 16 | Check with |A×B| = AB sin θ: 4\times4\times\sin 90^\circ = 16 | |A×B| is also the area of the parallelogram with sides A and B. \text{Area} = 16 | The direction is perpendicular to the plane of A and B (right-hand rule). The order matters: \vec{B}\times\vec{A} = -\vec{A}\times\vec{B} = -16\hat{k}  =>  A×B = 16\hat{k}, |A×B| = 16, θ = 90^\circ
op mag [4,0,0] [0,4,0]  =>  [Magnitude and direction of A] Given components: \vec{A} = 4\hat{i} | Magnitude: A = \sqrt{A_{x}^2 + A_{y}^2} = \sqrt{4^2 + 0^2} = \sqrt{16} = 4 | Direction: Ay = 0, so the vector points straight along the +x axis. \theta = 0^\circ  =>  |A| = 4, θ = 0^\circ
op resolve [4,0,0] [0,4,0]  =>  [Resolve A into rectangular components] Given: magnitude A = 4 at θ = 0° with the +x axis. | x-component: A_{x} = A\cos\theta = 4\cos 0^\circ = 4\times 1 = 4 | y-component: A_{y} = A\sin\theta = 4\sin 0^\circ = 4\times 0 = 0 | Written with unit vectors: \vec{A} = 4\hat{i}  =>  Ax = 4, Ay = 0
op scale [4,0,0] [0,4,0]  =>  [R = 2A] Multiply every component by the scalar: 2\,\vec{A} = 2(4\hat{i}) = 8\hat{i} | The magnitude is multiplied by |k|: \left|2\,\vec{A}\right| = |2|\times 4 = 8 | k is positive, so the direction does not change.  =>  R = 8\hat{i}, |R| = 8
op unit [4,0,0] [0,4,0]  =>  [Unit vector along A] A unit vector has magnitude 1 and points the same way as the vector. \hat{a} = \frac{\vec{A}}{A} | A = 4 | \hat{a} = \frac{4\hat{i}}{4} = 1\hat{i} | Check: its magnitude is \sqrt{1^2 + 0^2} = 1  =>  unit vector = 1\hat{i}
op angle [4,0,0] [0,4,0]  =>  [Angle between A and B] Given: \vec{A} = 4\hat{i},\quad \vec{B} = 4\hat{j} | Scalar product in terms of rectangular components: \vec{A}\cdot\vec{B} = A_{x}B_{x} + A_{y}B_{y} | = (4)(0) + (0)(4) = 0 | Magnitudes: A = 4,\quad B = 4 | Angle between them: \cos\theta = \frac{\vec{A}\cdot\vec{B}}{A\,B} = \frac{0}{4\times4} = 0 | \theta = \cos^{-1}(0) = 90^\circ | The dot product is zero, so the vectors are perpendicular (θ = 90°).  =>  θ = 90^\circ
op proj [4,0,0] [0,4,0]  =>  [Projection of B on A] Scalar projection of B on A (B cos θ): B\cos\theta = \frac{\vec{A}\cdot\vec{B}}{A} = \frac{0}{4} = 0 | Vector projection (the shadow of B along A): \text{proj}_{A}\vec{B} = \frac{\vec{A}\cdot\vec{B}}{A^2}\,\vec{A} = \frac{0}{16}(4\hat{i}) = \vec{0}  =>  B cos θ = 0, proj of B on A = \vec{0}
op equil [4,0,0] [0,4,0]  =>  [Force needed for equilibrium] Write every vector in component form: \vec{A} = 4\hat{i},\quad \vec{B} = 4\hat{j} | Add the x-components together, the y-components together. | R_{x} = A_{x} + B_{x} = 4 + 0 = 4 | R_{y} = A_{y} + B_{y} = 0 + 4 = 4 | Resultant vector: \vec{R} = 4\hat{i} + 4\hat{j} | Magnitude: R = \sqrt{R_{x}^2 + R_{y}^2} = \sqrt{4^2 + 4^2} = \sqrt{32} = 5.66 | Direction. First the reference angle, ignoring the signs: \theta_{ref} = \tan^{-1}\left(\frac{|R_{y}|}{|R_{x}|}\right) = \tan^{-1}\left(\frac{4}{4}\right) = 45^\circ | Rx is + and Ry is +, so the vector is in the 1st quadrant. \theta = \theta_{ref} = 45^\circ | For equilibrium the net force must be zero, so the balancing force (equilibrant) is equal and opposite to R: \vec{E} = -\vec{R} = -4\hat{i} - 4\hat{j} | \left|\vec{E}\right| = 5.66  =>  E = -4\hat{i} - 4\hat{j}, |E| = 5.66
op torque [4,0,0] [0,4,0]  =>  [Torque τ = r × F] Torque is the vector product of the position vector r and the force F. \vec{\tau} = \vec{r}\times\vec{F} | Given: \vec{r} = 4\hat{i},\quad \vec{F} = 4\hat{j} | Write the cross product as a determinant: \vec{r}\times\vec{F} = \begin{vmatrix} \hat{i} & \hat{j} & \hat{k} \\ 4 & 0 & 0 \\ 0 & 4 & 0 \end{vmatrix} | Expand along the first row: = \hat{i}\,[(0)(0) - (0)(4)] - \hat{j}\,[(4)(0) - (0)(0)] + \hat{k}\,[(4)(4) - (0)(0)] | \vec{τ} = 16\hat{k} | Magnitude: \left|\vec{τ}\right| = \sqrt{0^2 + 0^2 + 16^2} = \sqrt{256} = 16 | Check with |r×F| = rF sin θ: 4\times4\times\sin 90^\circ = 16 | |r×F| is also the area of the parallelogram with sides r and F. \text{Area} = 16 | The direction is perpendicular to the plane of r and F (right-hand rule). The order matters: \vec{F}\times\vec{r} = -\vec{r}\times\vec{F} = -16\hat{k}  =>  τ = 16\hat{k}\,\text{N m}, |τ| = 16\,\text{N m}, θ = 90^\circ
op work [4,0,0] [0,4,0]  =>  [Work done W = F · d] Work is the scalar product of force and displacement: W = F·d = Fd cos θ. | Given: \vec{F} = 4\hat{i},\quad \vec{d} = 4\hat{j} | Scalar product in terms of rectangular components: \vec{F}\cdot\vec{d} = F_{x}d_{x} + F_{y}d_{y} | = (4)(0) + (0)(4) = 0 | Magnitudes: F = 4,\quad d = 4 | Angle between them: \cos\theta = \frac{\vec{F}\cdot\vec{d}}{F\,d} = \frac{0}{4\times4} = 0 | \theta = \cos^{-1}(0) = 90^\circ | The dot product is zero, so the vectors are perpendicular (θ = 90°). | W = 0\,\text{J}  =>  W = 0\,\text{J}, θ = 90^\circ
op lorentz [4,0,0] [0,4,0]  =>  [Magnetic force F = q(v × B)] The force on a moving charge is F = q(v × B). First find v × B. | Given: \vec{v} = 4\hat{i},\quad \vec{B} = 4\hat{j} | Write the cross product as a determinant: \vec{v}\times\vec{B} = \begin{vmatrix} \hat{i} & \hat{j} & \hat{k} \\ 4 & 0 & 0 \\ 0 & 4 & 0 \end{vmatrix} | Expand along the first row: = \hat{i}\,[(0)(0) - (0)(4)] - \hat{j}\,[(4)(0) - (0)(0)] + \hat{k}\,[(4)(4) - (0)(0)] | \vec{v×B} = 16\hat{k} | Magnitude: \left|\vec{v×B}\right| = \sqrt{0^2 + 0^2 + 16^2} = \sqrt{256} = 16 | Multiply by the charge q = 1.6 × 10⁻¹⁹ C: \vec{F} = 1.6\times 10^{-19}\,(16\hat{k}) = \left(2.56\hat{k}\right)\times 10^{-18}\,\text{N} | \left|\vec{F}\right| = 2.56\times 10^{-18}\,\text{N}  =>  F = \left(2.56\hat{k}\right)\times 10^{-18}\,\text{N}, |F| = 2.56\times 10^{-18}\,\text{N}
op relvel [4,0,0] [0,4,0]  =>  [Velocity of A relative to B] Subtracting a vector means adding its negative (same magnitude, opposite direction): \vec{v_{A}} - \vec{v_{B}} = \vec{v_{A}} + (-\vec{v_{B}}) | -\vec{v_{B}} = -4\hat{j} | Add the x-components together, the y-components together. | v_{AB,x} = v_{A,x} + (-v_{B})_{x} = 4 + 0 = 4 | v_{AB,y} = v_{A,y} + (-v_{B})_{y} = 0 + (-4) = -4 | Resultant vector: \vec{v_{AB}} = 4\hat{i} - 4\hat{j} | Magnitude: v_{AB} = \sqrt{v_{AB,x}^2 + v_{AB,y}^2} = \sqrt{4^2 + (-4)^2} = \sqrt{32} = 5.66 | Direction. First the reference angle, ignoring the signs: \theta_{ref} = \tan^{-1}\left(\frac{|v_{AB,y}|}{|v_{AB,x}|}\right) = \tan^{-1}\left(\frac{4}{4}\right) = 45^\circ | v_ABx is + and v_ABy is −, so the vector is in the 4th quadrant. \theta = 360^\circ - \theta_{ref} = 360^\circ - 45^\circ = 315^\circ  =>  v_AB = 4\hat{i} - 4\hat{j}, |v_AB| = 5.66, θ = 315^\circ

op sum [4,0,0] [0,4.001,0]  =>  [Resultant R = A + B] Write every vector in component form: \vec{A} = 4\hat{i},\quad \vec{B} = 4\hat{j} | Add the x-components together, the y-components together. | R_{x} = A_{x} + B_{x} = 4 + 0 = 4 | R_{y} = A_{y} + B_{y} = 0 + 4 = 4 | Resultant vector: \vec{R} = 4\hat{i} + 4\hat{j} | Magnitude: R = \sqrt{R_{x}^2 + R_{y}^2} = \sqrt{4^2 + 4.001^2} = \sqrt{32.01} = 5.66 | Direction. First the reference angle, ignoring the signs: \theta_{ref} = \tan^{-1}\left(\frac{|R_{y}|}{|R_{x}|}\right) = \tan^{-1}\left(\frac{4.001}{4}\right) = 45.01^\circ | Rx is + and Ry is +, so the vector is in the 1st quadrant. \theta = \theta_{ref} = 45.01^\circ  =>  R = 4\hat{i} + 4\hat{j}, |R| = 5.66, θ = 45.01^\circ
op sub [4,0,0] [0,4.001,0]  =>  [R = A − B] Subtracting a vector means adding its negative (same magnitude, opposite direction): \vec{A} - \vec{B} = \vec{A} + (-\vec{B}) | -\vec{B} = -4\hat{j} | Add the x-components together, the y-components together. | R_{x} = A_{x} + (-B)_{x} = 4 + 0 = 4 | R_{y} = A_{y} + (-B)_{y} = 0 + (-4) = -4 | Resultant vector: \vec{R} = 4\hat{i} - 4\hat{j} | Magnitude: R = \sqrt{R_{x}^2 + R_{y}^2} = \sqrt{4^2 + (-4.001)^2} = \sqrt{32.01} = 5.66 | Direction. First the reference angle, ignoring the signs: \theta_{ref} = \tan^{-1}\left(\frac{|R_{y}|}{|R_{x}|}\right) = \tan^{-1}\left(\frac{4.001}{4}\right) = 45.01^\circ | Rx is + and Ry is −, so the vector is in the 4th quadrant. \theta = 360^\circ - \theta_{ref} = 360^\circ - 45.01^\circ = 314.99^\circ  =>  R = 4\hat{i} - 4\hat{j}, |R| = 5.66, θ = 314.99^\circ
op dot [4,0,0] [0,4.001,0]  =>  [Scalar product A·B] Given: \vec{A} = 4\hat{i},\quad \vec{B} = 4\hat{j} | Scalar product in terms of rectangular components: \vec{A}\cdot\vec{B} = A_{x}B_{x} + A_{y}B_{y} | = (4)(0) + (0)(4) = 0 | Magnitudes: A = 4,\quad B = 4 | Angle between them: \cos\theta = \frac{\vec{A}\cdot\vec{B}}{A\,B} = \frac{0}{4\times4} = 0 | \theta = \cos^{-1}(0) = 90^\circ | The dot product is zero, so the vectors are perpendicular (θ = 90°). | Meaning: A·B = |A| × (projection of B on A). B\cos\theta = 4\cos 90^\circ = 0  =>  A·B = 0, θ = 90^\circ
op cross [4,0,0] [0,4.001,0]  =>  [Vector product A×B] Given: \vec{A} = 4\hat{i},\quad \vec{B} = 4\hat{j} | Write the cross product as a determinant: \vec{A}\times\vec{B} = \begin{vmatrix} \hat{i} & \hat{j} & \hat{k} \\ 4 & 0 & 0 \\ 0 & 4 & 0 \end{vmatrix} | Expand along the first row: = \hat{i}\,[(0)(0) - (0)(4)] - \hat{j}\,[(4)(0) - (0)(0)] + \hat{k}\,[(4)(4) - (0)(0)] | \vec{C} = 16\hat{k} | Magnitude: \left|\vec{C}\right| = \sqrt{0^2 + 0^2 + 16.004^2} = \sqrt{256.13} = 16 | Check with |A×B| = AB sin θ: 4\times4\times\sin 90^\circ = 16 | |A×B| is also the area of the parallelogram with sides A and B. \text{Area} = 16 | The direction is perpendicular to the plane of A and B (right-hand rule). The order matters: \vec{B}\times\vec{A} = -\vec{A}\times\vec{B} = -16\hat{k}  =>  A×B = 16\hat{k}, |A×B| = 16, θ = 90^\circ
op mag [4,0,0] [0,4.001,0]  =>  [Magnitude and direction of A] Given components: \vec{A} = 4\hat{i} | Magnitude: A = \sqrt{A_{x}^2 + A_{y}^2} = \sqrt{4^2 + 0^2} = \sqrt{16} = 4 | Direction: Ay = 0, so the vector points straight along the +x axis. \theta = 0^\circ  =>  |A| = 4, θ = 0^\circ
op resolve [4,0,0] [0,4.001,0]  =>  [Resolve A into rectangular components] Given: magnitude A = 4 at θ = 0° with the +x axis. | x-component: A_{x} = A\cos\theta = 4\cos 0^\circ = 4\times 1 = 4 | y-component: A_{y} = A\sin\theta = 4\sin 0^\circ = 4\times 0 = 0 | Written with unit vectors: \vec{A} = 4\hat{i}  =>  Ax = 4, Ay = 0
op scale [4,0,0] [0,4.001,0]  =>  [R = 2A] Multiply every component by the scalar: 2\,\vec{A} = 2(4\hat{i}) = 8\hat{i} | The magnitude is multiplied by |k|: \left|2\,\vec{A}\right| = |2|\times 4 = 8 | k is positive, so the direction does not change.  =>  R = 8\hat{i}, |R| = 8

The order matters: \vec{B}\times\vec{A} = -\vec{A}\times\vec{B} = -\infty\hat{k}  =>  A×B = \infty\hat{k}, |A×B| = \infty, θ = 90^\circ
op mag [1e+200,0,0] [0,1e+200,0]  =>  [Magnitude and direction of A] Given components: \vec{A} = 1\times 10^{200}\hat{i} | Magnitude: A = \sqrt{A_{x}^2 + A_{y}^2} = \sqrt{(1\times 10^{200})^2 + 0^2} \approx \sqrt{\infty} \approx 1\times 10^{200} | Direction: Ay = 0, so the vector points straight along the +x axis. \theta = 0^\circ  =>  |A| = 1\times 10^{200}, θ = 0^\circ
op resolve [1e+200,0,0] [0,1e+200,0]  =>  [Resolve A into rectangular components] Given: magnitude A = 1 × 10²⁰⁰ at θ = 0° with the +x axis. | x-component: A_{x} = A\cos\theta = 1\times 10^{200}\cos 0^\circ = 1\times 10^{200}\times 1 = 1\times 10^{200} | y-component: A_{y} = A\sin\theta = 1\times 10^{200}\sin 0^\circ = 1\times 10^{200}\times 0 = 0 | Written with unit vectors: \vec{A} = 1\times 10^{200}\hat{i}  =>  Ax = 1\times 10^{200}, Ay = 0
op scale [1e+200,0,0] [0,1e+200,0]  =>  [R = 2A] Multiply every component by the scalar: 2\,\vec{A} = 2(1\times 10^{200}\hat{i}) = 2\times 10^{200}\hat{i} | The magnitude is multiplied by |k|: \left|2\,\vec{A}\right| = |2|\times 1\times 10^{200} = 2\times 10^{200} | k is positive, so the direction does not change.  =>  R = 2\times 10^{200}\hat{i}, |R| = 2\times 10^{200}
op unit [1e+200,0,0] [0,1e+200,0]  =>  [Unit vector along A] A unit vector has magnitude 1 and points the same way as the vector. \hat{a} = \frac{\vec{A}}{A} | A = 1\times 10^{200} | \hat{a} = \frac{1\times 10^{200}\hat{i}}{1\times 10^{200}} = 1\hat{i} | Check: its magnitude is \sqrt{1^2 + 0^2} = 1  =>  unit vector = 1\hat{i}
op angle [1e+200,0,0] [0,1e+200,0]  =>  [Angle between A and B] Given: \vec{A} = 1\times 10^{200}\hat{i},\quad \vec{B} = 1\times 10^{200}\hat{j} | Scalar product in terms of rectangular components: \vec{A}\cdot\vec{B} = A_{x}B_{x} + A_{y}B_{y} | = (1\times 10^{200})(0) + (0)(1\times 10^{200}) = 0 | Magnitudes: A = 1\times 10^{200},\quad B = 1\times 10^{200} | Angle between them: \cos\theta = \frac{\vec{A}\cdot\vec{B}}{A\,B} = \frac{0}{1\times 10^{200}\times1\times 10^{200}} = 0 | \theta = \cos^{-1}(0) = 90^\circ | The dot product is zero, so the vectors are perpendicular (θ = 90°).  =>  θ = 90^\circ
op proj [1e+200,0,0] [0,1e+200,0]  =>  [Projection of B on A] Scalar projection of B on A (B cos θ): B\cos\theta = \frac{\vec{A}\cdot\vec{B}}{A} = \frac{0}{1\times 10^{200}} = 0 | Vector projection (the shadow of B along A): \text{proj}_{A}\vec{B} = \frac{\vec{A}\cdot\vec{B}}{A^2}\,\vec{A} = \frac{0}{\infty}(1\times 10^{200}\hat{i}) \approx \vec{0}  =>  B cos θ = 0, proj of B on A = \vec{0}
op equil [1e+200,0,0] [0,1e+200,0]  =>  [Force needed for equilibrium] Write every vector in component form: \vec{A} = 1\times 10^{200}\hat{i},\quad \vec{B} = 1\times 10^{200}\hat{j} | Add the x-components together, the y-components together. | R_{x} = A_{x} + B_{x} = (1\times 10^{200}) + 0 = 1\times 10^{200} | R_{y} = A_{y} + B_{y} = 0 + (1\times 10^{200}) = 1\times 10^{200} | Resultant vector: \vec{R} = 1\times 10^{200}\hat{i} + 1\times 10^{200}\hat{j} | Magnitude: R = \sqrt{R_{x}^2 + R_{y}^2} = \sqrt{(1\times 10^{200})^2 + (1\times 10^{200})^2} \approx \sqrt{\infty} \approx 1.41\times 10^{200} | Direction. First the reference angle, ignoring the signs: \theta_{ref} = \tan^{-1}\left(\frac{|R_{y}|}{|R_{x}|}\right) = \tan^{-1}\left(\frac{1\times 10^{200}}{1\times 10^{200}}\right) = 45^\circ | Rx is + and Ry is +, so the vector is in the 1st quadrant. \theta = \theta_{ref} = 45^\circ | For equilibrium the net force must be zero, so the balancing force (equilibrant) is equal and opposite to R: \vec{E} = -\vec{R} = -1\times 10^{200}\hat{i} - 1\times 10^{200}\hat{j} | \left|\vec{E}\right| = 1.41\times 10^{200}  =>  E = -1\times 10^{200}\hat{i} - 1\times 10^{200}\hat{j}, |E| = 1.41\times 10^{200}
op torque [1e+200,0,0] [0,1e+200,0]  =>  [Torque τ = r × F] Torque is the vector product of the position vector r and the force F. \vec{\tau} = \vec{r}\times\vec{F} | Given: \vec{r} = 1\times 10^{200}\hat{i},\quad \vec{F} = 1\times 10^{200}\hat{j} | Write the cross product as a determinant: \vec{r}\times\vec{F} = \begin{vmatrix} \hat{i} & \hat{j} & \hat{k} \\ 1\times 10^{200} & 0 & 0 \\ 0 & 1\times 10^{200} & 0 \end{vmatrix} | Expand along the first row: = \hat{i}\,[(0)(0) - (0)(1\times 10^{200})] - \hat{j}\,[(1\times 10^{200})(0) - (0)(0)] + \hat{k}\,[(1\times 10^{200})(1\times 10^{200}) - (0)(0)] | \vec{τ} = \infty\hat{k} | Magnitude: \left|\vec{τ}\right| = \sqrt{0^2 + 0^2 + \infty^2} \approx \sqrt{\infty} \approx \infty | Check with |r×F| = rF sin θ: 1\times 10^{200}\times1\times 10^{200}\times\sin 90^\circ = \infty | |r×F| is also the area of the parallelogram with sides r and F. \text{Area} = \infty | The direction is perpendicular to the plane of r and F (right-hand rule). The order matters: \vec{F}\times\vec{r} = -\vec{r}\times\vec{F} = -\infty\hat{k}  =>  τ = \infty\hat{k}\,\text{N m}, |τ| = \infty\,\text{N m}, θ = 90^\circ
op work [1e+200,0,0] [0,1e+200,0]  =>  [Work done W = F · d] Work is the scalar product of force and displacement: W = F·d = Fd cos θ. | Given: \vec{F} = 1\times 10^{200}\hat{i},\quad \vec{d} = 1\times 10^{200}\hat{j} | Scalar product in terms of rectangular components: \vec{F}\cdot\vec{d} = F_{x}d_{x} + F_{y}d_{y} | = (1\times 10^{200})(0) + (0)(1\times 10^{200}) = 0 | Magnitudes: F = 1\times 10^{200},\quad d = 1\times 10^{200} | Angle between them: \cos\theta = \frac{\vec{F}\cdot\vec{d}}{F\,d} = \frac{0}{1\times 10^{200}\times1\times 10^{200}} = 0 | \theta = \cos^{-1}(0) = 90^\circ | The dot product is zero, so the vectors are perpendicular (θ = 90°). | W = 0\,\text{J}  =>  W = 0\,\text{J}, θ = 90^\circ
op lorentz [1e+200,0,0] [0,1e+200,0]  =>  [Magnetic force F = q(v × B)] The force on a moving charge is F = q(v × B). First find v × B. | Given: \vec{v} = 1\times 10^{200}\hat{i},\quad \vec{B} = 1\times 10^{200}\hat{j} | Write the cross product as a determinant: \vec{v}\times\vec{B} = \begin{vmatrix} \hat{i} & \hat{j} & \hat{k} \\ 1\times 10^{200} & 0 & 0 \\ 0 & 1\times 10^{200} & 0 \end{vmatrix} | Expand along the first row: = \hat{i}\,[(0)(0) - (0)(1\times 10^{200})] - \hat{j}\,[(1\times 10^{200})(0) - (0)(0)] + \hat{k}\,[(1\times 10^{200})(1\times 10^{200}) - (0)(0)] | \vec{v×B} = \infty\hat{k} | Magnitude: \left|\vec{v×B}\right| = \sqrt{0^2 + 0^2 + \infty^2} \approx \sqrt{\infty} \approx \infty | Multiply by the charge q = 1.6 × 10⁻¹⁹ C: \vec{F} = 1.6\times 10^{-19}\,(\infty\hat{k}) \approx \infty\hat{k}\,\text{N} | \left|\vec{F}\right| = \infty\,\text{N}  =>  F = \infty\hat{k}\,\text{N}, |F| = \infty\,\text{N}
op relvel [1e+200,0,0] [0,1e+200,0]  =>  [Velocity of A relative to B] Subtracting a vector means adding its negative (same magnitude, opposite direction): \vec{v_{A}} - \vec{v_{B}} = \vec{v_{A}} + (-\vec{v_{B}}) | -\vec{v_{B}} = -1\times 10^{200}\hat{j} | Add the x-components together, the y-components together. | v_{AB,x} = v_{A,x} + (-v_{B})_{x} = (1\times 10^{200}) + 0 = 1\times 10^{200} | v_{AB,y} = v_{A,y} + (-v_{B})_{y} = 0 + (-1\times 10^{200}) = -1\times 10^{200} | Resultant vector: \vec{v_{AB}} = 1\times 10^{200}\hat{i} - 1\times 10^{200}\hat{j} | Magnitude: v_{AB} = \sqrt{v_{AB,x}^2 + v_{AB,y}^2} = \sqrt{(1\times 10^{200})^2 + (-1\times 10^{200})^2} \approx \sqrt{\infty} \approx 1.41\times 10^{200} | Direction. First the reference angle, ignoring the signs: \theta_{ref} = \tan^{-1}\left(\frac{|v_{AB,y}|}{|v_{AB,x}|}\right) = \tan^{-1}\left(\frac{1\times 10^{200}}{1\times 10^{200}}\right) = 45^\circ | v_ABx is + and v_ABy is −, so the vector is in the 4th quadrant. \theta = 360^\circ - \theta_{ref} = 360^\circ - 45^\circ = 315^\circ  =>  v_AB = 1\times 10^{200}\hat{i} - 1\times 10^{200}\hat{j}, |v_AB| = 1.41\times 10^{200}, θ = 315^\circ
cardLineTex [1e+200,0,0]  =>  \vec{A} = {1\times 10^{200}\hat{i}}\quad |A| = {1\times 10^{200}\,\text{u}}\quad \theta = 0^\circ
op sum [1e+200,0,0] [1e+200,1e+200,0]  =>  [Resultant R = A + B] Write every vector in component form: \vec{A} = 1\times 10^{200}\hat{i},\quad \vec{B} = 1\times 10^{200}\hat{i} + 1\times 10^{200}\hat{j} | Add the x-components together, the y-components together. | R_{x} = A_{x} + B_{x} = (1\times 10^{200}) + (1\times 10^{200}) = 2\times 10^{200} | R_{y} = A_{y} + B_{y} = 0 + (1\times 10^{200}) = 1\times 10^{200} | Resultant vector: \vec{R} = 2\times 10^{200}\hat{i} + 1\times 10^{200}\hat{j} | Magnitude: R = \sqrt{R_{x}^2 + R_{y}^2} = \sqrt{(2\times 10^{200})^2 + (1\times 10^{200})^2} \approx \sqrt{\infty} \approx 2.24\times 10^{200} | Direction. First the reference angle, ignoring the signs: \theta_{ref} = \tan^{-1}\left(\frac{|R_{y}|}{|R_{x}|}\right) = \tan^{-1}\left(\frac{1\times 10^{200}}{2\times 10^{200}}\right) = 26.57^\circ | Rx is + and Ry is +, so the vector is in the 1st quadrant. \theta = \theta_{ref} = 26.57^\circ  =>  R = 2\times 10^{200}\hat{i} + 1\times 10^{200}\hat{j}, |R| = 2.24\times 10^{200}, θ = 26.57^\circ
op sub [1e+200,0,0] [1e+200,1e+200,0]  =>  [R = A − B] Subtracting a vector means adding its negative (same magnitude, opposite direction): \vec{A} - \vec{B} = \vec{A} + (-\vec{B}) | -\vec{B} = -1\times 10^{200}\hat{i} - 1\times 10^{200}\hat{j} | Add the x-components together, the y-components together. | R_{x} = A_{x} + (-B)_{x} = (1\times 10^{200}) + (-1\times 10^{200}) = 0 | R_{y} = A_{y} + (-B)_{y} = 0 + (-1\times 10^{200}) = -1\times 10^{200} | Resultant vector: \vec{R} = -1\times 10^{200}\hat{j} | Magnitude: R = \sqrt{R_{x}^2 + R_{y}^2} = \sqrt{0^2 + (-1\times 10^{200})^2} \approx \sqrt{\infty} \approx 1\times 10^{200} | Direction: Rx = 0, so the vector points straight along the −y axis. \theta = 270^\circ  =>  R = -1\times 10^{200}\hat{j}, |R| = 1\times 10^{200}, θ = 270^\circ
op dot [1e+200,0,0] [1e+200,1e+200,0]  =>  [Scalar product A·B] Given: \vec{A} = 1\times 10^{200}\hat{i},\quad \vec{B} = 1\times 10^{200}\hat{i} + 1\times 10^{200}\hat{j} | Scalar product in terms of rectangular components: \vec{A}\cdot\vec{B} = A_{x}B_{x} + A_{y}B_{y} | = (1\times 10^{200})(1\times 10^{200}) + (0)(1\times 10^{200}) = \infty | Magnitudes: A = 1\times 10^{200},\quad B = 1.41\times 10^{200} | Angle between them: \cos\theta = \frac{\vec{A}\cdot\vec{B}}{A\,B} = \frac{\infty}{1\times 10^{200}\times1.41\times 10^{200}} = \text{undefined} | \theta = \cos^{-1}(\text{undefined}) \approx 45^\circ | Meaning: A·B = |A| × (projection of B on A). op cross [1e+200,0,0] [1e+200,1e+200,0]  =>  [Vector product A×B] Given: \vec{A} = 1\times 10^{200}\hat{i},\quad \vec{B} = 1\times 10^{200}\hat{i} + 1\times 10^{200}\hat{j} | Write the cross product as a determinant: \vec{A}\times\vec{B} = \begin{vmatrix} \hat{i} & \hat{j} & \hat{k} \\ 1\times 10^{200} & 0 & 0 \\ 1\times 10^{200} & 1\times 10^{200} & 0 \end{vmatrix} | Expand along the first row: = \hat{i}\,[(0)(0) - (0)(1\times 10^{200})] - \hat{j}\,[(1\times 10^{200})(0) - (0)(1\times 10^{200})] + \hat{k}\,[(1\times 10^{200})(1\times 10^{200}) - (0)(1\times 10^{200})] | \vec{C} = \infty\hat{k} | Magnitude: \left|\vec{C}\right| = \sqrt{0^2 + 0^2 + \infty^2} \approx \sqrt{\infty} \approx \infty | Check with |A×B| = AB sin θ: 1\times 10^{200}\times1.41\times 10^{200}\times\sin 45^\circ = \infty | |A×B| is also the area of the parallelogram with sides A and B. \text{Area} = \infty | The direction is perpendicular to the plane of A and B (right-hand rule). op work [2,0,0] [5,0,0]  =>  [Work done W = F · d] Work is the scalar product of force and displacement: W = F·d = Fd cos θ. | Given: \vec{F} = 2\hat{i},\quad \vec{d} = 5\hat{i} | Scalar product in terms of rectangular components: \vec{F}\cdot\vec{d} = F_{x}d_{x} + F_{y}d_{y} | = (2)(5) + (0)(0) = 10 | Magnitudes: F = 2,\quad d = 5 | Angle between them: \cos\theta = \frac{\vec{F}\cdot\vec{d}}{F\,d} = \frac{10}{2\times5} = 1 | \theta = \cos^{-1}(1) = 0^\circ | W = 10\,\text{J}  =>  W = 10\,\text{J}, θ = 0^\circ
op lorentz [2,0,0] [5,0,0]  =>  [Magnetic force F = q(v × B)] The force on a moving charge is F = q(v × B). First find v × B. | Given: \vec{v} = 2\hat{i},\quad \vec{B} = 5\hat{i} | Write the cross product as a determinant: \vec{v}\times\vec{B} = \begin{vmatrix} \hat{i} & \hat{j} & \hat{k} \\ 2 & 0 & 0 \\ 5 & 0 & 0 \end{vmatrix} | Expand along the first row: = \hat{i}\,[(0)(0) - (0)(0)] - \hat{j}\,[(2)(0) - (0)(5)] + \hat{k}\,[(2)(0) - (0)(5)] | \vec{v×B} = \vec{0} | Magnitude: \left|\vec{v×B}\right| = \sqrt{0^2 + 0^2 + 0^2} = \sqrt{0} = 0 | Multiply by the charge q = 1.6 × 10⁻¹⁹ C: \vec{F} = 1.6\times 10^{-19}\,(\vec{0}) = \vec{0}\,\text{N} | \left|\vec{F}\right| = 0\,\text{N}  =>  F = \vec{0}\,\text{N}, |F| = 0\,\text{N}
op relvel [2,0,0] [5,0,0]  =>  [Velocity of A relative to B] Subtracting a vector means adding its negative (same magnitude, opposite direction): \vec{v_{A}} - \vec{v_{B}} = \vec{v_{A}} + (-\vec{v_{B}}) | -\vec{v_{B}} = -5\hat{i} | Add the x-components together, the y-components together. | v_{AB,x} = v_{A,x} + (-v_{B})_{x} = 2 + (-5) = -3 | v_{AB,y} = v_{A,y} + (-v_{B})_{y} = 0 + 0 = 0 | Resultant vector: \vec{v_{AB}} = -3\hat{i} | Magnitude: v_{AB} = \sqrt{v_{AB,x}^2 + v_{AB,y}^2} = \sqrt{(-3)^2 + 0^2} = \sqrt{9} = 3 | Direction: v_ABy = 0, so the vector points straight along the −x axis. \theta = 180^\circ  =>  v_AB = -3\hat{i}, |v_AB| = 3, θ = 180^\circ
cardLineTex [2,0,0]  =>  \vec{A} = 2\hat{i}\quad |A| = 2\,\text{u}\quad \theta = 0^\circ
op sum [2,0,0] [-5,0,0]  =>  [Resultant R = A + B] Write every vector in component form: \vec{A} = 2\hat{i},\quad \vec{B} = -5\hat{i} | Add the x-components together, the y-components together. | R_{x} = A_{x} + B_{x} = 2 + (-5) = -3 | R_{y} = A_{y} + B_{y} = 0 + 0 = 0 | Resultant vector: \vec{R} = -3\hat{i} | Magnitude: R = \sqrt{R_{x}^2 + R_{y}^2} = \sqrt{(-3)^2 + 0^2} = \sqrt{9} = 3 | Direction: Ry = 0, so the vector points straight along the −x axis. \theta = 180^\circ  =>  R = -3\hat{i}, |R| = 3, θ = 180^\circ
op sub [2,0,0] [-5,0,0]  =>  [R = A − B] Subtracting a vector means adding its negative (same magnitude, opposite direction): \vec{A} - \vec{B} = \vec{A} + (-\vec{B}) | -\vec{B} = 5\hat{i} | Add the x-components together, the y-components together. | R_{x} = A_{x} + (-B)_{x} = 2 + 5 = 7 | R_{y} = A_{y} + (-B)_{y} = 0 + 0 = 0 | Resultant vector: \vec{R} = 7\hat{i} | Magnitude: R = \sqrt{R_{x}^2 + R_{y}^2} = \sqrt{7^2 + 0^2} = \sqrt{49} = 7 | Direction: Ry = 0, so the vector points straight along the +x axis. \theta = 0^\circ  =>  R = 7\hat{i}, |R| = 7, θ = 0^\circ
op dot [2,0,0] [-5,0,0]  =>  [Scalar product A·B] Given: \vec{A} = 2\hat{i},\quad \vec{B} = -5\hat{i} | Scalar product in terms of rectangular components: \vec{A}\cdot\vec{B} = A_{x}B_{x} + A_{y}B_{y} | = (2)(-5) + (0)(0) = -10 | Magnitudes: A = 2,\quad B = 5 | Angle between them: \cos\theta = \frac{\vec{A}\cdot\vec{B}}{A\,B} = \frac{-10}{2\times5} = -1 | \theta = \cos^{-1}(-1) = 180^\circ | Meaning: A·B = |A| × (projection of B on A). B\cos\theta = 5\cos 180^\circ = -5  =>  A·B = -10, θ = 180^\circ
op cross [2,0,0] [-5,0,0]  =>  [Vector product A×B] Given: \vec{A} = 2\hat{i},\quad \vec{B} = -5\hat{i} | Write the cross product as a determinant: \vec{A}\times\vec{B} = \begin{vmatrix} \hat{i} & \hat{j} & \hat{k} \\ 2 & 0 & 0 \\ -5 & 0 & 0 \end{vmatrix} | Expand along the first row: = \hat{i}\,[(0)(0) - (0)(0)] - \hat{j}\,[(2)(0) - (0)(-5)] + \hat{k}\,[(2)(0) - (0)(-5)] | \vec{C} = \vec{0} | Magnitude: \left|\vec{C}\right| = \sqrt{0^2 + 0^2 + 0^2} = \sqrt{0} = 0 | Check with |A×B| = AB sin θ: 2\times5\times\sin 180^\circ = 0 | |A×B| is also the area of the parallelogram with sides A and B. \text{Area} = 0 | A×B is the null vector, which has no direction. Swapping the order changes nothing: \vec{B}\times\vec{A} = -\vec{A}\times\vec{B} = \vec{0} | θ = 180°, so the vectors are antiparallel.  =>  A×B = \vec{0}, |A×B| = 0, θ = 180^\circ
op mag [2,0,0] [-5,0,0]  =>  [Magnitude and direction of A] Given components: \vec{A} = 2\hat{i} | Magnitude: A = \sqrt{A_{x}^2 + A_{y}^2} = \sqrt{2^2 + 0^2} = \sqrt{4} = 2 | Direction: Ay = 0, so the vector points straight along the +x axis. \theta = 0^\circ  =>  |A| = 2, θ = 0^\circ
op resolve [2,0,0] [-5,0,0]  =>  [Resolve A into rectangular components] Given: magnitude A = 2 at θ = 0° with the +x axis. | x-component: A_{x} = A\cos\theta = 2\cos 0^\circ = 2\times 1 = 2 | y-component: A_{y} = A\sin\theta = 2\sin 0^\circ = 2\times 0 = 0 | Written with unit vectors: \vec{A} = 2\hat{i}  =>  Ax = 2, Ay = 0
op scale [2,0,0] [-5,0,0]  =>  [R = 2A] Multiply every component by the scalar: 2\,\vec{A} = 2(2\hat{i}) = 4\hat{i} | The magnitude is multiplied by |k|: \left|2\,\vec{A}\right| = |2|\times 2 = 4 | k is positive, so the direction does not change.  =>  R = 4\hat{i}, |R| = 4
op unit [2,0,0] [-5,0,0]  =>  [Unit vector along A] A unit vector has magnitude 1 and points the same way as the vector. \hat{a} = \frac{\vec{A}}{A} | A = 2 | \hat{a} = \frac{2\hat{i}}{2} = 1\hat{i} | Check: its magnitude is \sqrt{1^2 + 0^2} = 1  =>  unit vector = 1\hat{i}
op angle [2,0,0] [-5,0,0]  =>  [Angle between A and B] Given: \vec{A} = 2\hat{i},\quad \vec{B} = -5\hat{i} | Scalar product in terms of rectangular components: \vec{A}\cdot\vec{B} = A_{x}B_{x} + A_{y}B_{y} | = (2)(-5) + (0)(0) = -10 | Magnitudes: A = 2,\quad B = 5 | Angle between them: \cos\theta = \frac{\vec{A}\cdot\vec{B}}{A\,B} = \frac{-10}{2\times5} = -1 | \theta = \cos^{-1}(-1) = 180^\circ | The vectors point opposite ways (θ = 180°): they are antiparallel.  =>  θ = 180^\circ
op proj [2,0,0] [-5,0,0]  =>  [Projection of B on A] Scalar projection of B on A (B cos θ): B\cos\theta = \frac{\vec{A}\cdot\vec{B}}{A} = \frac{-10}{2} = -5 | Vector projection (the shadow of B along A): \text{proj}_{A}\vec{B} = \frac{\vec{A}\cdot\vec{B}}{A^2}\,\vec{A} = \frac{-10}{4}(2\hat{i}) = -5\hat{i}  =>  B cos θ = -5, proj of B on A = -5\hat{i}
op equil [2,0,0] [-5,0,0]  =>  [Force needed for equilibrium] Write every vector in component form: \vec{A} = 2\hat{i},\quad \vec{B} = -5\hat{i} | Add the x-components together, the y-components together. | R_{x} = A_{x} + B_{x} = 2 + (-5) = -3 | R_{y} = A_{y} + B_{y} = 0 + 0 = 0 | Resultant vector: \vec{R} = -3\hat{i} | Magnitude: R = \sqrt{R_{x}^2 + R_{y}^2} = \sqrt{(-3)^2 + 0^2} = \sqrt{9} = 3 | Direction: Ry = 0, so the vector points straight along the −x axis. \theta = 180^\circ | For equilibrium the net force must be zero, so the balancing force (equilibrant) is equal and opposite to R: \vec{E} = -\vec{R} = 3\hat{i} | \left|\vec{E}\right| = 3  =>  E = 3\hat{i}, |E| = 3
op torque [2,0,0] [-5,0,0]  =>  [Torque τ = r × F] Torque is the vector product of the position vector r and the force F. \vec{\tau} = \vec{r}\times\vec{F} | Given: \vec{r} = 2\hat{i},\quad \vec{F} = -5\hat{i} | Write the cross product as a determinant: \vec{r}\times\vec{F} = \begin{vmatrix} \hat{i} & \hat{j} & \hat{k} \\ 2 & 0 & 0 \\ -5 & 0 & 0 \end{vmatrix} | Expand along the first row: = \hat{i}\,[(0)(0) - (0)(0)] - \hat{j}\,[(2)(0) - (0)(-5)] + \hat{k}\,[(2)(0) - (0)(-5)] | \vec{τ} = \vec{0} | Magnitude: \left|\vec{τ}\right| = \sqrt{0^2 + 0^2 + 0^2} = \sqrt{0} = 0 | Check with |r×F| = rF sin θ: 2\times5\times\sin 180^\circ = 0 | |r×F| is also the area of the parallelogram with sides r and F. \text{Area} = 0 | r×F is the null vector, which has no direction. op work [2,0,0] [-5,0,0]  =>  [Work done W = F · d] Work is the scalar product of force and displacement: W = F·d = Fd cos θ. | Given: \vec{F} = 2\hat{i},\quad \vec{d} = -5\hat{i} | Scalar product in terms of rectangular components: \vec{F}\cdot\vec{d} = F_{x}d_{x} + F_{y}d_{y} | = (2)(-5) + (0)(0) = -10 | Magnitudes: F = 2,\quad d = 5 | Angle between them: \cos\theta = \frac{\vec{F}\cdot\vec{d}}{F\,d} = \frac{-10}{2\times5} = -1 | \theta = \cos^{-1}(-1) = 180^\circ | W = -10\,\text{J}  =>  W = -10\,\text{J}, θ = 180^\circ

First find v × B. | Given: \vec{v} = 2\hat{i},\quad \vec{B} = -5\hat{i} | Write the cross product as a determinant: \vec{v}\times\vec{B} = \begin{vmatrix} \hat{i} & \hat{j} & \hat{k} \\ 2 & 0 & 0 \\ -5 & 0 & 0 \end{vmatrix} | Expand along the first row: = \hat{i}\,[(0)(0) - (0)(0)] - \hat{j}\,[(2)(0) - (0)(-5)] + \hat{k}\,[(2)(0) - (0)(-5)] | \vec{v×B} = \vec{0} | Magnitude: \left|\vec{v×B}\right| = \sqrt{0^2 + 0^2 + 0^2} = \sqrt{0} = 0 | Multiply by the charge q = 1.6 × 10⁻¹⁹ C: \vec{F} = 1.6\times 10^{-19}\,(\vec{0}) = \vec{0}\,\text{N} | \left|\vec{F}\right| = 0\,\text{N}  =>  F = \vec{0}\,\text{N}, |F| = 0\,\text{N}

op sum [1e-15,0,0] [0,1e-15,0]  =>  [Resultant R = A + B] Write every vector in component form: \vec{A} = \left(1\hat{i}\right)\times 10^{-15},\quad \vec{B} = \left(1\hat{j}\right)\times 10^{-15} | Add the x-components together, the y-components together. | R_{x} = A_{x} + B_{x} = 1\times 10^{-15} + 0 = 1\times 10^{-15} | R_{y} = A_{y} + B_{y} = 0 + 1\times 10^{-15} = 1\times 10^{-15} | Resultant vector: \vec{R} = \left(1\hat{i} + 1\hat{j}\right)\times 10^{-15} | Magnitude: R = \sqrt{R_{x}^2 + R_{y}^2} = \sqrt{(1\times 10^{-15})^2 + (1\times 10^{-15})^2} = \sqrt{2\times 10^{-30}} = 1.41\times 10^{-15} | Direction. First the reference angle, ignoring the signs: \theta_{ref} = \tan^{-1}\left(\frac{|R_{y}|}{|R_{x}|}\right) = \tan^{-1}\left(\frac{1\times 10^{-15}}{1\times 10^{-15}}\right) = 45^\circ | Rx is + and Ry is +, so the vector is in the 1st quadrant. \theta = \theta_{ref} = 45^\circ  =>  R = \left(1\hat{i} + 1\hat{j}\right)\times 10^{-15}, |R| = 1.41\times 10^{-15}, θ = 45^\circ
op sub [1e-15,0,0] [0,1e-15,0]  =>  [R = A − B] Subtracting a vector means adding its negative (same magnitude, opposite direction): \vec{A} - \vec{B} = \vec{A} + (-\vec{B}) | -\vec{B} = \left(-1\hat{j}\right)\times 10^{-15} | Add the x-components together, the y-components together. | R_{x} = A_{x} + (-B)_{x} = 1\times 10^{-15} + 0 = 1\times 10^{-15} | R_{y} = A_{y} + (-B)_{y} = 0 + (-1\times 10^{-15}) = -1\times 10^{-15} | Resultant vector: \vec{R} = \left(1\hat{i} - 1\hat{j}\right)\times 10^{-15} | Magnitude: R = \sqrt{R_{x}^2 + R_{y}^2} = \sqrt{(1\times 10^{-15})^2 + (-1\times 10^{-15})^2} = \sqrt{2\times 10^{-30}} = 1.41\times 10^{-15} | Direction. First the reference angle, ignoring the signs: \theta_{ref} = \tan^{-1}\left(\frac{|R_{y}|}{|R_{x}|}\right) = \tan^{-1}\left(\frac{1\times 10^{-15}}{1\times 10^{-15}}\right) = 45^\circ | Rx is + and Ry is −, so the vector is in the 4th quadrant. \theta = 360^\circ - \theta_{ref} = 360^\circ - 45^\circ = 315^\circ  =>  R = \left(1\hat{i} - 1\hat{j}\right)\times 10^{-15}, |R| = 1.41\times 10^{-15}, θ = 315^\circ
op dot [1e-15,0,0] [0,1e-15,0]  =>  [Scalar product A·B] Given: \vec{A} = \left(1\hat{i}\right)\times 10^{-15},\quad \vec{B} = \left(1\hat{j}\right)\times 10^{-15} | Scalar product in terms of rectangular components: \vec{A}\cdot\vec{B} = A_{x}B_{x} + A_{y}B_{y} | = (1\times 10^{-15})(0) + (0)(1\times 10^{-15}) = 0 | Magnitudes: A = 1\times 10^{-15},\quad B = 1\times 10^{-15} | Angle between them: \cos\theta = \frac{\vec{A}\cdot\vec{B}}{A\,B} = \frac{0}{1\times 10^{-15}\times1\times 10^{-15}} = 0 | \theta = \cos^{-1}(0) = 90^\circ | The dot product is zero, so the vectors are perpendicular (θ = 90°). | Meaning: A·B = |A| × (projection of B on A). B\cos\theta = 1\times 10^{-15}\cos 90^\circ = 0  =>  A·B = 0, θ = 90^\circ
op cross [1e-15,0,0] [0,1e-15,0]  =>  [Vector product A×B] Given: \vec{A} = \left(1\hat{i}\right)\times 10^{-15},\quad \vec{B} = \left(1\hat{j}\right)\times 10^{-15} | Write the cross product as a determinant: \vec{A}\times\vec{B} = \begin{vmatrix} \hat{i} & \hat{j} & \hat{k} \\ 1\times 10^{-15} & 0 & 0 \\ 0 & 1\times 10^{-15} & 0 \end{vmatrix} | Expand along the first row: = \hat{i}\,[(0)(0) - (0)(1\times 10^{-15})] - \hat{j}\,[(1\times 10^{-15})(0) - (0)(0)] + \hat{k}\,[(1\times 10^{-15})(1\times 10^{-15}) - (0)(0)] | \vec{C} = \left(1\hat{k}\right)\times 10^{-30} | Magnitude: \left|\vec{C}\right| = \sqrt{0^2 + 0^2 + (1\times 10^{-30})^2} = \sqrt{1\times 10^{-60}} = 1\times 10^{-30} | Check with |A×B| = AB sin θ: 1\times 10^{-15}\times1\times 10^{-15}\times\sin 90^\circ = 1\times 10^{-30} | |A×B| is also the area of the parallelogram with sides A and B. \text{Area} = 1\times 10^{-30} | The direction is perpendicular to the plane of A and B (right-hand rule). The order matters: \vec{B}\times\vec{A} = -\vec{A}\times\vec{B} = \left(-1\hat{k}\right)\times 10^{-30}  =>  A×B = \left(1\hat{k}\right)\times 10^{-30}, |A×B| = 1\times 10^{-30}, θ = 90^\circ
op mag [1e-15,0,0] [0,1e-15,0]  =>  [Magnitude and direction of A] Given components: \vec{A} = \left(1\hat{i}\right)\times 10^{-15} | Magnitude: A = \sqrt{A_{x}^2 + A_{y}^2} = \sqrt{(1\times 10^{-15})^2 + 0^2} = \sqrt{1\times 10^{-30}} = 1\times 10^{-15} | Direction: Ay = 0, so the vector points straight along the +x axis. \theta = 0^\circ  =>  |A| = 1\times 10^{-15}, θ = 0^\circ
op resolve [1e-15,0,0] [0,1e-15,0]  =>  [Resolve A into rectangular components] Given: magnitude A = 1 × 10⁻¹⁵ at θ = 0° with the +x axis. | x-component: A_{x} = A\cos\theta = 1\times 10^{-15}\cos 0^\circ = 1\times 10^{-15}\times 1 = 1\times 10^{-15} | y-component: A_{y} = A\sin\theta = 1\times 10^{-15}\sin 0^\circ = 1\times 10^{-15}\times 0 = 0 | Written with unit vectors: \vec{A} = \left(1\hat{i}\right)\times 10^{-15}  =>  Ax = 1\times 10^{-15}, Ay = 0
op scale [1e-15,0,0] [0,1e-15,0]  =>  [R = 2A] Multiply every component by the scalar: 2\,\vec{A} = 2(\left(1\hat{i}\right)\times 10^{-15}) = \left(2\hat{i}\right)\times 10^{-15} | The magnitude is multiplied by |k|: \left|2\,\vec{A}\right| = |2|\times 1\times 10^{-15} = 2\times 10^{-15} | k is positive, so the direction does not change.  =>  R = \left(2\hat{i}\right)\times 10^{-15}, |R| = 2\times 10^{-15}
op unit [1e-15,0,0] [0,1e-15,0]  =>  [Unit vector along A] A unit vector has magnitude 1 and points the same way as the vector. \hat{a} = \frac{\vec{A}}{A} | A = 1\times 10^{-15} | \hat{a} = \frac{\left(1\hat{i}\right)\times 10^{-15}}{1\times 10^{-15}} = 1\hat{i} | Check: its magnitude is \sqrt{1^2 + 0^2} = 1  =>  unit vector = 1\hat{i}
op angle [1e-15,0,0] [0,1e-15,0]  =>  [Angle between A and B] Given: \vec{A} = \left(1\hat{i}\right)\times 10^{-15},\quad \vec{B} = \left(1\hat{j}\right)\times 10^{-15} | Scalar product in terms of rectangular components: \vec{A}\cdot\vec{B} = A_{x}B_{x} + A_{y}B_{y} | = (1\times 10^{-15})(0) + (0)(1\times 10^{-15}) = 0 | Magnitudes: A = 1\times 10^{-15},\quad B = 1\times 10^{-15} | Angle between them: \cos\theta = \frac{\vec{A}\cdot\vec{B}}{A\,B} = \frac{0}{1\times 10^{-15}\times1\times 10^{-15}} = 0 | \theta = \cos^{-1}(0) = 90^\circ | The dot product is zero, so the vectors are perpendicular (θ = 90°).  =>  θ = 90^\circ
op proj [1e-15,0,0] [0,1e-15,0]  =>  [Projection of B on A] Scalar projection of B on A (B cos θ): B\cos\theta = \frac{\vec{A}\cdot\vec{B}}{A} = \frac{0}{1\times 10^{-15}} = 0 | Vector projection (the shadow of B along A): \text{proj}_{A}\vec{B} = \frac{\vec{A}\cdot\vec{B}}{A^2}\,\vec{A} = \frac{0}{1\times 10^{-30}}(\left(1\hat{i}\right)\times 10^{-15}) = \vec{0}  =>  B cos θ = 0, proj of B on A = \vec{0}
op equil [1e-15,0,0] [0,1e-15,0]  =>  [Force needed for equilibrium] Write every vector in component form: \vec{A} = \left(1\hat{i}\right)\times 10^{-15},\quad \vec{B} = \left(1\hat{j}\right)\times 10^{-15} | Add the x-components together, the y-components together. | R_{x} = A_{x} + B_{x} = 1\times 10^{-15} + 0 = 1\times 10^{-15} | R_{y} = A_{y} + B_{y} = 0 + 1\times 10^{-15} = 1\times 10^{-15} | Resultant vector: \vec{R} = \left(1\hat{i} + 1\hat{j}\right)\times 10^{-15} | Magnitude: R = \sqrt{R_{x}^2 + R_{y}^2} = \sqrt{(1\times 10^{-15})^2 + (1\times 10^{-15})^2} = \sqrt{2\times 10^{-30}} = 1.41\times 10^{-15} | Direction. First the reference angle, ignoring the signs: \theta_{ref} = \tan^{-1}\left(\frac{|R_{y}|}{|R_{x}|}\right) = \tan^{-1}\left(\frac{1\times 10^{-15}}{1\times 10^{-15}}\right) = 45^\circ | Rx is + and Ry is +, so the vector is in the 1st quadrant. \theta = \theta_{ref} = 45^\circ | For equilibrium the net force must be zero, so the balancing force (equilibrant) is equal and opposite to R: \vec{E} = -\vec{R} = \left(-1\hat{i} - 1\hat{j}\right)\times 10^{-15} | \left|\vec{E}\right| = 1.41\times 10^{-15}  =>  E = \left(-1\hat{i} - 1\hat{j}\right)\times 10^{-15}, |E| = 1.41\times 10^{-15}
op torque [1e-15,0,0] [0,1e-15,0]  =>  [Torque τ = r × F] Torque is the vector product of the position vector r and the force F. \vec{\tau} = \vec{r}\times\vec{F} | Given: \vec{r} = \left(1\hat{i}\right)\times 10^{-15},\quad \vec{F} = \left(1\hat{j}\right)\times 10^{-15} | Write the cross product as a determinant: \vec{r}\times\vec{F} = \begin{vmatrix} \hat{i} & \hat{j} & \hat{k} \\ 1\times 10^{-15} & 0 & 0 \\ 0 & 1\times 10^{-15} & 0 \end{vmatrix} | Expand along the first row: = \hat{i}\,[(0)(0) - (0)(1\times 10^{-15})] - \hat{j}\,[(1\times 10^{-15})(0) - (0)(0)] + \hat{k}\,[(1\times 10^{-15})(1\times 10^{-15}) - (0)(0)] | \vec{τ} = \left(1\hat{k}\right)\times 10^{-30} | Magnitude: \left|\vec{τ}\right| = \sqrt{0^2 + 0^2 + (1\times 10^{-30})^2} = \sqrt{1\times 10^{-60}} = 1\times 10^{-30} | Check with |r×F| = rF sin θ: 1\times 10^{-15}\times1\times 10^{-15}\times\sin 90^\circ = 1\times 10^{-30} | |r×F| is also the area of the parallelogram with sides r and F. \text{Area} = 1\times 10^{-30} | The direction is perpendicular to the plane of r and F (right-hand rule). The order matters: \vec{F}\times\vec{r} = -\vec{r}\times\vec{F} = \left(-1\hat{k}\right)\times 10^{-30}  =>  τ = \left(1\hat{k}\right)\times 10^{-30}\,\text{N m}, |τ| = 1\times 10^{-30}\,\text{N m}, θ = 90^\circ
op work [1e-15,0,0] [0,1e-15,0]  =>  [Work done W = F · d] Work is the scalar product of force and displacement: W = F·d = Fd cos θ. | Given: \vec{F} = \left(1\hat{i}\right)\times 10^{-15},\quad \vec{d} = \left(1\hat{j}\right)\times 10^{-15} | Scalar product in terms of rectangular components: \vec{F}\cdot\vec{d} = F_{x}d_{x} + F_{y}d_{y} | = (1\times 10^{-15})(0) + (0)(1\times 10^{-15}) = 0 | Magnitudes: F = 1\times 10^{-15},\quad d = 1\times 10^{-15} | Angle between them: \cos\theta = \frac{\vec{F}\cdot\vec{d}}{F\,d} = \frac{0}{1\times 10^{-15}\times1\times 10^{-15}} = 0 | \theta = \cos^{-1}(0) = 90^\circ | The dot product is zero, so the vectors are perpendicular (θ = 90°). | W = 0\,\text{J}  =>  W = 0\,\text{J}, θ = 90^\circ
op lorentz [1e-15,0,0] [0,1e-15,0]  =>  [Magnetic force F = q(v × B)] The force on a moving charge is F = q(v × B). First find v × B. | Given: \vec{v} = \left(1\hat{i}\right)\times 10^{-15},\quad \vec{B} = \left(1\hat{j}\right)\times 10^{-15} | Write the cross product as a determinant: \vec{v}\times\vec{B} = \begin{vmatrix} \hat{i} & \hat{j} & \hat{k} \\ 1\times 10^{-15} & 0 & 0 \\ 0 & 1\times 10^{-15} & 0 \end{vmatrix} | Expand along the first row: = \hat{i}\,[(0)(0) - (0)(1\times 10^{-15})] - \hat{j}\,[(1\times 10^{-15})(0) - (0)(0)] + \hat{k}\,[(1\times 10^{-15})(1\times 10^{-15}) - (0)(0)] | \vec{v×B} = \left(1\hat{k}\right)\times 10^{-30} | Magnitude: \left|\vec{v×B}\right| = \sqrt{0^2 + 0^2 + (1\times 10^{-30})^2} = \sqrt{1\times 10^{-60}} = 1\times 10^{-30} | Multiply by the charge q = 1.6 × 10⁻¹⁹ C: \vec{F} = 1.6\times 10^{-19}\,(\left(1\hat{k}\right)\times 10^{-30}) = \left(1.6\hat{k}\right)\times 10^{-49}\,\text{N} | \left|\vec{F}\right| = 1.6\times 10^{-49}\,\text{N}  =>  F = \left(1.6\hat{k}\right)\times 10^{-49}\,\text{N}, |F| = 1.6\times 10^{-49}\,\text{N}
op relvel [1e-15,0,0] [0,1e-15,0]  =>  [Velocity of A relative to B] Subtracting a vector means adding its negative (same magnitude, opposite direction): \vec{v_{A}} - \vec{v_{B}} = \vec{v_{A}} + (-\vec{v_{B}}) | -\vec{v_{B}} = \left(-1\hat{j}\right)\times 10^{-15} | Add the x-components together, the y-components together. | v_{AB,x} = v_{A,x} + (-v_{B})_{x} = 1\times 10^{-15} + 0 = 1\times 10^{-15} | v_{AB,y} = v_{A,y} + (-v_{B})_{y} = 0 + (-1\times 10^{-15}) = -1\times 10^{-15} | Resultant vector: \vec{v_{AB}} = \left(1\hat{i} - 1\hat{j}\right)\times 10^{-15} | Magnitude: v_{AB} = \sqrt{v_{AB,x}^2 + v_{AB,y}^2} = \sqrt{(1\times 10^{-15})^2 + (-1\times 10^{-15})^2} = \sqrt{2\times 10^{-30}} = 1.41\times 10^{-15} | Direction. First the reference angle, ignoring the signs: \theta_{ref} = \tan^{-1}\left(\frac{|v_{AB,y}|}{|v_{AB,x}|}\right) = \tan^{-1}\left(\frac{1\times 10^{-15}}{1\times 10^{-15}}\right) = 45^\circ | v_ABx is + and v_ABy is −, so the vector is in the 4th quadrant. \theta = 360^\circ - \theta_{ref} = 360^\circ - 45^\circ = 315^\circ  =>  v_AB = \left(1\hat{i} - 1\hat{j}\right)\times 10^{-15}, |v_AB| = 1.41\times 10^{-15}, θ = 315^\circ
cardLineTex [1e-20,0,0]  =>  \vec{A} = {1\times 10^{-20}\hat{i}}\quad |A| = {1\times 10^{-20}\,\text{u}}\quad \theta = 0^\circ
op sum [1e-20,0,0] [1e-20,1e-20,0]  =>  [Resultant R = A + B] Write every vector in component form: \vec{A} = \left(1\hat{i}\right)\times 10^{-20},\quad \vec{B} = \left(1\hat{i} + 1\hat{j}\right)\times 10^{-20} | Add the x-components together, the y-components together. | R_{x} = A_{x} + B_{x} = 1\times 10^{-20} + 1\times 10^{-20} = 2\times 10^{-20} | R_{y} = A_{y} + B_{y} = 0 + 1\times 10^{-20} = 1\times 10^{-20} | Resultant vector: \vec{R} = \left(2\hat{i} + 1\hat{j}\right)\times 10^{-20} | Magnitude: R = \sqrt{R_{x}^2 + R_{y}^2} = \sqrt{(2\times 10^{-20})^2 + (1\times 10^{-20})^2} = \sqrt{5\times 10^{-40}} = 2.24\times 10^{-20} | Direction. First the reference angle, ignoring the signs: \theta_{ref} = \tan^{-1}\left(\frac{|R_{y}|}{|R_{x}|}\right) = \tan^{-1}\left(\frac{1\times 10^{-20}}{2\times 10^{-20}}\right) = 26.57^\circ | Rx is + and Ry is +, so the vector is in the 1st quadrant. \theta = \theta_{ref} = 26.57^\circ  =>  R = \left(2\hat{i} + 1\hat{j}\right)\times 10^{-20}, |R| = 2.24\times 10^{-20}, θ = 26.57^\circ
op sub [1e-20,0,0] [1e-20,1e-20,0]  =>  [R = A − B] Subtracting a vector means adding its negative (same magnitude, opposite direction): \vec{A} - \vec{B} = \vec{A} + (-\vec{B}) | -\vec{B} = \left(-1\hat{i} - 1\hat{j}\right)\times 10^{-20} | Add the x-components together, the y-components together. | R_{x} = A_{x} + (-B)_{x} = 1\times 10^{-20} + (-1\times 10^{-20}) = 0 | R_{y} = A_{y} + (-B)_{y} = 0 + (-1\times 10^{-20}) = -1\times 10^{-20} | Resultant vector: \vec{R} = \left(-1\hat{j}\right)\times 10^{-20} | Magnitude: R = \sqrt{R_{x}^2 + R_{y}^2} = \sqrt{0^2 + (-1\times 10^{-20})^2} = \sqrt{1\times 10^{-40}} = 1\times 10^{-20} | Direction: Rx = 0, so the vector points straight along the −y axis. \theta = 270^\circ  =>  R = \left(-1\hat{j}\right)\times 10^{-20}, |R| = 1\times 10^{-20}, θ = 270^\circ
op dot [1e-20,0,0] [1e-20,1e-20,0]  =>  [Scalar product A·B] Given: \vec{A} = \left(1\hat{i}\right)\times 10^{-20},\quad \vec{B} = \left(1\hat{i} + 1\hat{j}\right)\times 10^{-20} | Scalar product in terms of rectangular components: \vec{A}\cdot\vec{B} = A_{x}B_{x} + A_{y}B_{y} | = (1\times 10^{-20})(1\times 10^{-20}) + (0)(1\times 10^{-20}) = 1\times 10^{-40} | Magnitudes: A = 1\times 10^{-20},\quad B = 1.41\times 10^{-20} | Angle between them: \cos\theta = \frac{\vec{A}\cdot\vec{B}}{A\,B} = \frac{1\times 10^{-40}}{1\times 10^{-20}\times1.4142\times 10^{-20}} = 0.7071 | \theta = \cos^{-1}(0.7071) = 45^\circ | Meaning: A·B = |A| × (projection of B on A). B\cos\theta = 1.414\times 10^{-20}\cos 45^\circ = 1\times 10^{-20}  =>  A·B = 1\times 10^{-40}, θ = 45^\circ
op cross [1e-20,0,0] [1e-20,1e-20,0]  =>  [Vector product A×B] Given: \vec{A} = \left(1\hat{i}\right)\times 10^{-20},\quad \vec{B} = \left(1\hat{i} + 1\hat{j}\right)\times 10^{-20} | Write the cross product as a determinant: \vec{A}\times\vec{B} = \begin{vmatrix} \hat{i} & \hat{j} & \hat{k} \\ 1\times 10^{-20} & 0 & 0 \\ 1\times 10^{-20} & 1\times 10^{-20} & 0 \end{vmatrix} | Expand along the first row: = \hat{i}\,[(0)(0) - (0)(1\times 10^{-20})] - \hat{j}\,[(1\times 10^{-20})(0) - (0)(1\times 10^{-20})] + \hat{k}\,[(1\times 10^{-20})(1\times 10^{-20}) - (0)(1\times 10^{-20})] | \vec{C} = \left(1\hat{k}\right)\times 10^{-40} | Magnitude: \left|\vec{C}\right| = \sqrt{0^2 + 0^2 + (1\times 10^{-40})^2} = \sqrt{1\times 10^{-80}} = 1\times 10^{-40} | Check with |A×B| = AB sin θ: 1\times 10^{-20}\times1.414\times 10^{-20}\times\sin 45^\circ = 1\times 10^{-40} | |A×B| is also the area of the parallelogram with sides A and B. \text{Area} = 1\times 10^{-40} | The direction is perpendicular to the plane of A and B (right-hand rule). The order matters: \vec{B}\times\vec{A} = -\vec{A}\times\vec{B} = \left(-1\hat{k}\right)\times 10^{-40}  =>  A×B = \left(1\hat{k}\right)\times 10^{-40}, |A×B| = 1\times 10^{-40}, θ = 45^\circ
op mag [1e-20,0,0] [1e-20,1e-20,0]  =>  [Magnitude and direction of A] Given components: \vec{A} = \left(1\hat{i}\right)\times 10^{-20} | Magnitude: A = \sqrt{A_{x}^2 + A_{y}^2} = \sqrt{(1\times 10^{-20})^2 + 0^2} = \sqrt{1\times 10^{-40}} = 1\times 10^{-20} | Direction: Ay = 0, so the vector points straight along the +x axis. \theta = 0^\circ  =>  |A| = 1\times 10^{-20}, θ = 0^\circ
op resolve [1e-20,0,0] [1e-20,1e-20,0]  =>  [Resolve A into rectangular components] Given: magnitude A = 1 × 10⁻²⁰ at θ = 0° with the +x axis. | x-component: A_{x} = A\cos\theta = 1\times 10^{-20}\cos 0^\circ = 1\times 10^{-20}\times 1 = 1\times 10^{-20} | y-component: A_{y} = A\sin\theta = 1\times 10^{-20}\sin 0^\circ = 1\times 10^{-20}\times 0 = 0 | Written with unit vectors: \vec{A} = \left(1\hat{i}\right)\times 10^{-20}  =>  Ax = 1\times 10^{-20}, Ay = 0
op scale [1e-20,0,0] [1e-20,1e-20,0]  =>  [R = 2A] Multiply every component by the scalar: 2\,\vec{A} = 2(\left(1\hat{i}\right)\times 10^{-20}) = \left(2\hat{i}\right)\times 10^{-20} | The magnitude is multiplied by |k|: \left|2\,\vec{A}\right| = |2|\times 1\times 10^{-20} = 2\times 10^{-20} | k is positive, so the direction does not change.  =>  R = \left(2\hat{i}\right)\times 10^{-20}, |R| = 2\times 10^{-20}
op unit [1e-20,0,0] [1e-20,1e-20,0]  =>  [Unit vector along A] A unit vector has magnitude 1 and points the same way as the vector. \hat{a} = \frac{\vec{A}}{A} | A = 1\times 10^{-20} | \hat{a} = \frac{\left(1\hat{i}\right)\times 10^{-20}}{1\times 10^{-20}} = 1\hat{i} | Check: its magnitude is \sqrt{1^2 + 0^2} = 1  =>  unit vector = 1\hat{i}
op angle [1e-20,0,0] [1e-20,1e-20,0]  =>  [Angle between A and B] Given: \vec{A} = \left(1\hat{i}\right)\times 10^{-20},\quad \vec{B} = \left(1\hat{i} + 1\hat{j}\right)\times 10^{-20} | Scalar product in terms of rectangular components: \vec{A}\cdot\vec{B} = A_{x}B_{x} + A_{y}B_{y} | = (1\times 10^{-20})(1\times 10^{-20}) + (0)(1\times 10^{-20}) = 1\times 10^{-40} | Magnitudes: A = 1\times 10^{-20},\quad B = 1.41\times 10^{-20} | Angle between them: \cos\theta = \frac{\vec{A}\cdot\vec{B}}{A\,B} = \frac{1\times 10^{-40}}{1\times 10^{-20}\times1.4142\times 10^{-20}} = 0.7071 | \theta = \cos^{-1}(0.7071) = 45^\circ  =>  θ = 45^\circ
op proj [1e-20,0,0] [1e-20,1e-20,0]  =>  [Projection of B on A] Scalar projection of B on A (B cos θ): B\cos\theta = \frac{\vec{A}\cdot\vec{B}}{A} = \frac{1\times 10^{-40}}{1\times 10^{-20}} = 1\times 10^{-20} | Vector projection (the shadow of B along A): \text{proj}_{A}\vec{B} = \frac{\vec{A}\cdot\vec{B}}{A^2}\,\vec{A} = \frac{1\times 10^{-40}}{1\times 10^{-40}}(\left(1\hat{i}\right)\times 10^{-20}) = \left(1\hat{i}\right)\times 10^{-20}  =>  B cos θ = 1\times 10^{-20}, proj of B on A = \left(1\hat{i}\right)\times 10^{-20}

op sum [1,0,0] [1,1e-15,0]  =>  [Resultant R = A + B] Write every vector in component form: \vec{A} = 1\hat{i},\quad \vec{B} = 1\hat{i} | Add the x-components together, the y-components together. | R_{x} = A_{x} + B_{x} = 1 + 1 = 2 | R_{y} = A_{y} + B_{y} = 0 + 0 = 0 | Resultant vector: \vec{R} = 2\hat{i} | Magnitude: R = \sqrt{R_{x}^2 + R_{y}^2} = \sqrt{2^2 + 0^2} = \sqrt{4} = 2 | Direction: Ry = 0, so the vector points straight along the +x axis. \theta = 0^\circ  =>  R = 2\hat{i}, |R| = 2, θ = 0^\circ
op sub [1,0,0] [1,1e-15,0]  =>  [R = A − B] Subtracting a vector means adding its negative (same magnitude, opposite direction): \vec{A} - \vec{B} = \vec{A} + (-\vec{B}) | -\vec{B} = -1\hat{i} | Add the x-components together, the y-components together. | R_{x} = A_{x} + (-B)_{x} = 1 + (-1) = 0 | R_{y} = A_{y} + (-B)_{y} = 0 + 0 = 0 | Resultant vector: \vec{R} = \vec{0} | Magnitude: R = \sqrt{R_{x}^2 + R_{y}^2} = \sqrt{0^2 + 0^2} = \sqrt{0} = 0 | Direction: the vector has zero length, so its direction is undefined.  =>  R = \vec{0}, |R| = 0, θ = \text{undefined}
op dot [1,0,0] [1,1e-15,0]  =>  [Scalar product A·B] Given: \vec{A} = 1\hat{i},\quad \vec{B} = 1\hat{i} | Scalar product in terms of rectangular components: \vec{A}\cdot\vec{B} = A_{x}B_{x} + A_{y}B_{y} | = (1)(1) + (0)(0) = 1 | Magnitudes: A = 1,\quad B = 1 | Angle between them: \cos\theta = \frac{\vec{A}\cdot\vec{B}}{A\,B} = \frac{1}{1\times1} = 1 | \theta = \cos^{-1}(1) = 0^\circ | Meaning: A·B = |A| × (projection of B on A). B\cos\theta = 1\cos 0^\circ = 1  =>  A·B = 1, θ = 0^\circ
op cross [1,0,0] [1,1e-15,0]  =>  [Vector product A×B] Given: \vec{A} = 1\hat{i},\quad \vec{B} = 1\hat{i} | Write the cross product as a determinant: \vec{A}\times\vec{B} = \begin{vmatrix} \hat{i} & \hat{j} & \hat{k} \\ 1 & 0 & 0 \\ 1 & 0 & 0 \end{vmatrix} | Expand along the first row: = \hat{i}\,[(0)(0) - (0)(0)] - \hat{j}\,[(1)(0) - (0)(1)] + \hat{k}\,[(1)(0) - (0)(1)] | \vec{C} = \vec{0} | Magnitude: \left|\vec{C}\right| = \sqrt{0^2 + 0^2 + 0^2} = \sqrt{0} = 0 | Check with |A×B| = AB sin θ: 1\times1\times\sin 0^\circ = 0 | |A×B| is also the area of the parallelogram with sides A and B. \text{Area} = 0 | A×B is the null vector, which has no direction. Swapping the order changes nothing: \vec{B}\times\vec{A} = -\vec{A}\times\vec{B} = \vec{0} | θ = 0°, so the vectors are parallel.  =>  A×B = \vec{0}, |A×B| = 0, θ = 0^\circ
op mag [1,0,0] [1,1e-15,0]  =>  [Magnitude and direction of A] Given components: \vec{A} = 1\hat{i} | Magnitude: A = \sqrt{A_{x}^2 + A_{y}^2} = \sqrt{1^2 + 0^2} = \sqrt{1} = 1 | Direction: Ay = 0, so the vector points straight along the +x axis. \theta = 0^\circ  =>  |A| = 1, θ = 0^\circ
op resolve [1,0,0] [1,1e-15,0]  =>  [Resolve A into rectangular components] Given: magnitude A = 1 at θ = 0° with the +x axis. | x-component: A_{x} = A\cos\theta = 1\cos 0^\circ = 1\times 1 = 1 | y-component: A_{y} = A\sin\theta = 1\sin 0^\circ = 1\times 0 = 0 | Written with unit vectors: \vec{A} = 1\hat{i}  =>  Ax = 1, Ay = 0
op scale [1,0,0] [1,1e-15,0]  =>  [R = 2A] Multiply every component by the scalar: 2\,\vec{A} = 2(1\hat{i}) = 2\hat{i} | The magnitude is multiplied by |k|: \left|2\,\vec{A}\right| = |2|\times 1 = 2 | k is positive, so the direction does not change.  =>  R = 2\hat{i}, |R| = 2
op unit [1,0,0] [1,1e-15,0]  =>  [Unit vector along A] A unit vector has magnitude 1 and points the same way as the vector. \hat{a} = \frac{\vec{A}}{A} | A = 1 | \hat{a} = \frac{1\hat{i}}{1} = 1\hat{i} | Check: its magnitude is \sqrt{1^2 + 0^2} = 1  =>  unit vector = 1\hat{i}
op angle [1,0,0] [1,1e-15,0]  =>  [Angle between A and B] Given: \vec{A} = 1\hat{i},\quad \vec{B} = 1\hat{i} | Scalar product in terms of rectangular components: \vec{A}\cdot\vec{B} = A_{x}B_{x} + A_{y}B_{y} | = (1)(1) + (0)(0) = 1 | Magnitudes: A = 1,\quad B = 1 | Angle between them: \cos\theta = \frac{\vec{A}\cdot\vec{B}}{A\,B} = \frac{1}{1\times1} = 1 | \theta = \cos^{-1}(1) = 0^\circ | The vectors point the same way (θ = 0°): they are parallel.  =>  θ = 0^\circ
op proj [1,0,0] [1,1e-15,0]  =>  [Projection of B on A] Scalar projection of B on A (B cos θ): B\cos\theta = \frac{\vec{A}\cdot\vec{B}}{A} = \frac{1}{1} = 1 | Vector projection (the shadow of B along A): \text{proj}_{A}\vec{B} = \frac{\vec{A}\cdot\vec{B}}{A^2}\,\vec{A} = \frac{1}{1}(1\hat{i}) = 1\hat{i}  =>  B cos θ = 1, proj of B on A = 1\hat{i}
op equil [1,0,0] [1,1e-15,0]  =>  [Force needed for equilibrium] Write every vector in component form: \vec{A} = 1\hat{i},\quad \vec{B} = 1\hat{i} | Add the x-components together, the y-components together. | R_{x} = A_{x} + B_{x} = 1 + 1 = 2 | R_{y} = A_{y} + B_{y} = 0 + 0 = 0 | Resultant vector: \vec{R} = 2\hat{i} | Magnitude: R = \sqrt{R_{x}^2 + R_{y}^2} = \sqrt{2^2 + 0^2} = \sqrt{4} = 2 | Direction: Ry = 0, so the vector points straight along the +x axis. \theta = 0^\circ | For equilibrium the net force must be zero, so the balancing force (equilibrant) is equal and opposite to R: \vec{E} = -\vec{R} = -2\hat{i} | \left|\vec{E}\right| = 2  =>  E = -2\hat{i}, |E| = 2
op torque [1,0,0] [1,1e-15,0]  =>  [Torque τ = r × F] Torque is the vector product of the position vector r and the force F. \vec{\tau} = \vec{r}\times\vec{F} | Given: \vec{r} = 1\hat{i},\quad \vec{F} = 1\hat{i} | Write the cross product as a determinant: \vec{r}\times\vec{F} = \begin{vmatrix} \hat{i} & \hat{j} & \hat{k} \\ 1 & 0 & 0 \\ 1 & 0 & 0 \end{vmatrix} | Expand along the first row: = \hat{i}\,[(0)(0) - (0)(0)] - \hat{j}\,[(1)(0) - (0)(1)] + \hat{k}\,[(1)(0) - (0)(1)] | \vec{τ} = \vec{0} | Magnitude: \left|\vec{τ}\right| = \sqrt{0^2 + 0^2 + 0^2} = \sqrt{0} = 0 | Check with |r×F| = rF sin θ: 1\times1\times\sin 0^\circ = 0 | |r×F| is also the area of the parallelogram with sides r and F. \text{Area} = 0 | r×F is the null vector, which has no direction. Swapping the order changes nothing: \vec{F}\times\vec{r} = -\vec{r}\times\vec{F} = \vec{0} | θ = 0°, so the vectors are parallel.  =>  τ = \vec{0}\,\text{N m}, |τ| = 0\,\text{N m}, θ = 0^\circ
op work [1,0,0] [1,1e-15,0]  =>  [Work done W = F · d] Work is the scalar product of force and displacement: W = F·d = Fd cos θ. | Given: \vec{F} = 1\hat{i},\quad \vec{d} = 1\hat{i} | Scalar product in terms of rectangular components: \vec{F}\cdot\vec{d} = F_{x}d_{x} + F_{y}d_{y} | = (1)(1) + (0)(0) = 1 | Magnitudes: F = 1,\quad d = 1 | Angle between them: \cos\theta = \frac{\vec{F}\cdot\vec{d}}{F\,d} = \frac{1}{1\times1} = 1 | \theta = \cos^{-1}(1) = 0^\circ | W = 1\,\text{J}  =>  W = 1\,\text{J}, θ = 0^\circ
op lorentz [1,0,0] [1,1e-15,0]  =>  [Magnetic force F = q(v × B)] The force on a moving charge is F = q(v × B). First find v × B. | Given: \vec{v} = 1\hat{i},\quad \vec{B} = 1\hat{i} | Write the cross product as a determinant: \vec{v}\times\vec{B} = \begin{vmatrix} \hat{i} & \hat{j} & \hat{k} \\ 1 & 0 & 0 \\ 1 & 0 & 0 \end{vmatrix} | Expand along the first row: = \hat{i}\,[(0)(0) - (0)(0)] - \hat{j}\,[(1)(0) - (0)(1)] + \hat{k}\,[(1)(0) - (0)(1)] | \vec{v×B} = \vec{0} | Magnitude: \left|\vec{v×B}\right| = \sqrt{0^2 + 0^2 + 0^2} = \sqrt{0} = 0 | Multiply by the charge q = 1.6 × 10⁻¹⁹ C: \vec{F} = 1.6\times 10^{-19}\,(\vec{0}) = \vec{0}\,\text{N} | \left|\vec{F}\right| = 0\,\text{N}  =>  F = \vec{0}\,\text{N}, |F| = 0\,\text{N}
op relvel [1,0,0] [1,1e-15,0]  =>  [Velocity of A relative to B] Subtracting a vector means adding its negative (same magnitude, opposite direction): \vec{v_{A}} - \vec{v_{B}} = \vec{v_{A}} + (-\vec{v_{B}}) | -\vec{v_{B}} = -1\hat{i} | Add the x-components together, the y-components together. | v_{AB,x} = v_{A,x} + (-v_{B})_{x} = 1 + (-1) = 0 | v_{AB,y} = v_{A,y} + (-v_{B})_{y} = 0 + 0 = 0 | Resultant vector: \vec{v_{AB}} = \vec{0} | Magnitude: v_{AB} = \sqrt{v_{AB,x}^2 + v_{AB,y}^2} = \sqrt{0^2 + 0^2} = \sqrt{0} = 0 | Direction: the vector has zero length, so its direction is undefined.  =>  v_AB = \vec{0}, |v_AB| = 0, θ = \text{undefined}
exprSolution \vec{A} + \vec{B}  =>  [Resultant of the expression] Evaluate component by component: \vec{R} = \vec{A} + \vec{B} = 5\hat{i} + 3\hat{j} | Magnitude: R = \sqrt{R_{x}^2 + R_{y}^2} = \sqrt{5^2 + 3^2} = \sqrt{34} = 5.83 | Direction. First the reference angle, ignoring the signs: \theta_{ref} = \tan^{-1}\left(\frac{|R_{y}|}{|R_{x}|}\right) = \tan^{-1}\left(\frac{3}{5}\right) = 30.96^\circ | Rx is + and Ry is +, so the vector is in the 1st quadrant. \theta = \theta_{ref} = 30.96^\circ  =>  R = 5\hat{i} + 3\hat{j}, |R| = 5.83, θ = 30.96^\circ
exprSolution \vec{A} - \vec{B}  =>  [Resultant of the expression] Evaluate component by component: \vec{R} = \vec{A} - \vec{B} = 1\hat{i} + 5\hat{j} | Magnitude: R = \sqrt{R_{x}^2 + R_{y}^2} = \sqrt{1^2 + 5^2} = \sqrt{26} = 5.1 | Direction. First the reference angle, ignoring the signs: \theta_{ref} = \tan^{-1}\left(\frac{|R_{y}|}{|R_{x}|}\right) = \tan^{-1}\left(\frac{5}{1}\right) = 78.69^\circ | Rx is + and Ry is +, so the vector is in the 1st quadrant. \theta = \theta_{ref} = 78.69^\circ  =>  R = 1\hat{i} + 5\hat{j}, |R| = 5.1, θ = 78.69^\circ
exprSolution 2\vec{A}  =>  [Resultant of the expression] Evaluate component by component: \vec{R} = 2\vec{A} = 6\hat{i} + 8\hat{j} | Magnitude: R = \sqrt{R_{x}^2 + R_{y}^2} = \sqrt{6^2 + 8^2} = \sqrt{100} = 10 | Direction. First the reference angle, ignoring the signs: \theta_{ref} = \tan^{-1}\left(\frac{|R_{y}|}{|R_{x}|}\right) = \tan^{-1}\left(\frac{8}{6}\right) = 53.13^\circ | Rx is + and Ry is +, so the vector is in the 1st quadrant. \theta = \theta_{ref} = 53.13^\circ  =>  R = 6\hat{i} + 8\hat{j}, |R| = 10, θ = 53.13^\circ

exprSolution \vec{A} \times \vec{B}  =>  [Resultant of the expression] Evaluate component by component: \vec{R} = \vec{A} \times \vec{B} = -11\hat{k} | Magnitude (Pythagorean theorem in 3D): R = \sqrt{R_{x}^2 + R_{y}^2 + R_{z}^2} = \sqrt{0^2 + 0^2 + (-11)^2} = \sqrt{121} = 11 | Angle with the x-axis (direction cosine): \alpha = \cos^{-1}\left(\frac{R_{x}}{R}\right) = \cos^{-1}\left(\frac{0}{11}\right) = 90^\circ | Angle with the y-axis (direction cosine): \beta = \cos^{-1}\left(\frac{R_{y}}{R}\right) = \cos^{-1}\left(\frac{0}{11}\right) = 90^\circ | Angle with the z-axis (direction cosine): \gamma = \cos^{-1}\left(\frac{R_{z}}{R}\right) = \cos^{-1}\left(\frac{-11}{11}\right) = 180^\circ  =>  R = -11\hat{k}, |R| = 11, α, β, γ = 90^\circ,\ 90^\circ,\ 180^\circ
exprSolution |\vec{A}|  =>  [Result] |\vec{A}| = 5  =>  value = 5

First the reference angle, ignoring the signs: \theta_{ref} = \tan^{-1}\left(\frac{|R_{y}|}{|R_{x}|}\right) = \tan^{-1}\left(\frac{2}{1.5}\right) = 53.13^\circ | Rx is + and Ry is +, so the vector is in the 1st quadrant. \theta = \theta_{ref} = 53.13^\circ  =>  R = 1.5\hat{i} + 2\hat{j}, |R| = 2.5, θ = 53.13^\circ
